\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{l=1,l\neq j}^{N}\nabla^{2}V(X_{t+u}^{t,x_{i},i,N}-X_{t+u}^{t,x_{l},l,N})~(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{t+u,x_{j}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{t+u,x_{j}})\bigg|^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg|\nabla^{2}V(X_{t+u}^{t,x_{i},i,N}-X_{t+u}^{t,x_{j},j,N})~(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{t+u,x_{j}}-X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{t+u,x_{j}})\bigg|^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{N}\sum_{l=1,l\neq j}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\nabla^{2}V(X_{t+u}^{t,x_{i},i,N}-X_{t+u}^{t,x_{l},l,N})~(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{t+u,x_{j}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{t+u,x_{j}})\Big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{K}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{t+u,x_{j}}-X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{t+u,x_{j}}\Big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{K}{N}\sum_{i=1,i\neq j}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{t+u,x_{j}}|^{2}\Big]+\frac{K}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{t+u,x_{j}}|^{2}\Big]\leq\frac{K}{N^{2}}e^{-2\lambda_{1}u}},

where we isolated the
l=j
term, applied
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2(a^{2}+b^{2})
for
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, before applying Jensen’s inequality and Assumption
1
. Therefore, for the summation term in (
4.33
):

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,i\neq j}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{j}}-X^{s,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{j}}|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq K(s-t)\int_{0}^{s-t}\bigg(\sum_{i=1,i\neq j}^{N}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\nabla^{2}V(X_{t+u}^{t,x_{i},i,N}-X_{t+u}^{t,x_{l},l,N})~(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{t+u,x_{j}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{t+u,x_{j}})\bigg|^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\sum_{i=1,i\neq j}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|\nabla^{2}U(X_{t+u}^{t,x_{i},i,N})|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{t+u,x_{j}}|^{2}\Big]~\bigg)~\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\leq K(s-t)\int_{0}^{s-t}\bigg(\Big(\sum_{i=1,i\neq j}^{N}\frac{e^{-2\lambda_{1}u}}{N^{2}}\Big)+\frac{e^{-2\lambda_{1}u}}{N}\bigg)~\mathrm{d}u\leq\frac{K(s-t)}{N}\int_{0}^{s-t}e^{-2\lambda_{1}u}\mathrm{d}u\leq\frac{K(s-t)}{N},

(4.34)

where we used Lemma
4.2
in the last line. Consequently, substituting (
4.34
) into (
4.33
), we conclude

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,i\neq j}^{N}\mathbb{E}\big[\,|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}-X^{s,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}\big]\leq\frac{K(s-t)}{N}e^{-2\lambda_{3}(T-s)}.

∎

4.2
Second Variation process

Let
T\geq s\geq t\geq 0
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
. The second variation process of
(\bm{X}^{t,\bm{x},N}_{s})_{s\geq t\geq 0}

is defined, for
i,j,k\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, as

\displaystyle X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{j},x_{k}}

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{s}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\partial_{x_{l}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{u}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\int_{t}^{s}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{N}\partial^{2}_{x_{l},x_{l^{\prime}}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{u}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{l^{\prime}},l^{\prime},N}_{u,x_{k}}\mathrm{d}u.

(4.35)

The following lemma proceeds the results in Lemma
4.2
and accounts for the different behaviours of
L^{p}
-moments for the second order variation processes defined in (
4.35
), which is needed in Lemma
4.5
and contributes to the analysis in Section
6
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let Assumption
2
hold and let
p\geq 2
.
Consider the second variation process (
4.35
) and
assume that the starting positions
x_{i}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathbb{R})
are
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable random variables that are identically distributed over all
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.
Then there exist
\lambda_{4}\in(0,\min\{\lambda-(2+1/N)K_{V},\lambda_{3}\})
and
K>0
(both independent of
s,t,T
and
N
) such that for any
T\geq s\geq t\geq 0
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{i},x_{i}}|^{p}\Big]\leq Ke^{-\lambda_{4}p(s-t)},\qquad\quad\sum_{i,j,k=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{N^{2p-3}}e^{-\lambda_{4}p(s-t)},

\displaystyle\textrm{and}\quad\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{k},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{k},x_{i}}|^{p}\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{N^{p-2}}e^{-\lambda_{4}p(s-t)}.

Remark 4.2
.

This lemma continues to highlight the main difficulty faced in this manuscript’s analysis. The above inequalities suggest that the second-order variation process, where
\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k))=1
, i.e.,
i=j=k
, yields the
\mathcal{O}(1)
behaviour, while all other elements (i.e., the cross-derivatives with
\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k))\geq 2
) decay differently with respect to the number of particles. We refer to Lemma
4.5
for a general result.

Proof.

Let
p\geq 2
be a given integer. Note that in the following proof, the positive constant
K
is independent of
s,t,T,N
and may change line by line.

Part 1: Preliminary manipulations.
For
\lambda_{4}\in(0,\min\{\lambda-(2+1/N)K_{V},\lambda_{3}\})
, we define
I_{t,s}^{2,p}
for all
s\geq t\geq 0
as

\displaystyle I_{t,s}^{2,p}:=e^{p\lambda_{4}(s-t)}\bigg(\frac{1}{N}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{i},x_{i}}|^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle+N^{p-2}\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{k},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{k},x_{i}}|^{p}\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+N^{2p-3}\sum_{i,j,k=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]\bigg),

(4.36)

for which we will aim to show that we can upper bound
I_{t,s}^{2,p}\leq K
. We start by analysing each of the second variation processes:
For any
i,j,k\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
,
\lambda_{4}\in(0,\min\{\lambda-(2+1/N)K_{V},\lambda_{3}\})
,
s\geq t\geq 0
, we have that

\displaystyle e^{p\lambda_{4}(s-t)}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle=-p\int_{t}^{s}e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\big(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}\big)\cdot\big(\nabla^{2}U(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u})X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}\big)|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p-2}\bigg]\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\quad-p\int_{t}^{s}e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{N}\partial_{x_{l}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{q=1}^{N}\nabla V(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u}-X^{t,x_{q},q,N}_{u})X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}\Big)|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p-2}\bigg]\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\quad+p\int_{t}^{s}\lambda_{4}e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\quad+p\int_{t}^{s}e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{N}\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{N}\partial_{x_{l},x_{l^{\prime}}}^{2}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{u}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{l^{\prime}},l^{\prime},N}_{u,x_{k}}\Big)|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p-2}\bigg]\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\leq p\int_{t}^{s}(-\lambda+\lambda_{4})e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]\mathrm{d}u

(4.37)

\displaystyle\quad-p\int_{t}^{s}e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{l=1}^{N}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nabla^{2}V}(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u})(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}})\Big)

(4.38)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\cdot|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p-2}\bigg]\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\quad+p\int_{t}^{s}e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p-1}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{N}|\partial_{x_{l},x_{l^{\prime}}}^{2}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{u}^{t,\bm{x},N})|~|X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{j}}|~|X^{t,x_{l^{\prime}},l^{\prime},N}_{u,x_{k}}|\Big]\mathrm{d}u,

(4.39)

where we used Assumption
1
on the first term to obtain (
4.37
). In what follows, the last two terms, which we will refer to as the convolution term (
4.38
) and the lower order variation term (
4.39
), will be investigated in more detail.

Part 2: Analysis of the convolution term (
4.38
).

We analyze the convolution term (
4.38
) by considering five different cases:
i=j=k,~i\neq j=k,~~i=j\neq k,~~i=k\neq j
and
i\neq j\neq k
, where we will see that different methodologies need to be implemented based on the values
\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k))
of the second variation processes. Note that
i\neq j\neq k
indicates that none of the indices are identical.

Case
i=j=k
:
The convolution term, after summing over all
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, simplifies to

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\Big(|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u})(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}})\Big)\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i,l=1,~i\neq l}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u})X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i,l=1,~i\neq l}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u})X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{K_{V}}{N}\sum_{i,l=1,~i\neq l}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}|^{p-1}\ \cdot|X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}|\Big]}

(4.40)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K_{V}(p-1)}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}|^{p}\Big]+\frac{K_{V}}{Np}\sum_{i,l=1,~i\neq l}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}|^{p}\Big],

(4.41)

where we used
\nabla^{2}V(x)\geq 0
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and Assumption
1
to derive (
4.40
) and (
4.41
) is a consequence of Young’s inequality.

Case
i\neq j=k
:
In this situation, after summing over all
i,k\in\{1,\ldots,N\},~i\neq k
and splitting the summation over
l
, we derive using the
symmetrization trick

that

\displaystyle-\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\Big(|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u})(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}})\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{i,k,l=1,~i,l\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}-|X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u})(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\Big(|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u}-X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{u})(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}-X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}})\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K_{V}\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}|^{p-1}\cdot\frac{|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}|}{N}\Big]}

(4.42)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K_{V}(p-1)}{p}\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\frac{K_{V}}{pN^{p-1}}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big],

(4.43)

where we used
\nabla^{2}V(x)\geq 0
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and Assumption
1
to derive (
4.42
). (
4.43
) is a consequence of Young’s inequality.

Cases

i=j\neq k
and
i=k\neq j
: These two cases share similar calculations and we show the first of these. Summing over all
i,k\in\{1,\ldots,N\},~i\neq k
, we have

\displaystyle-\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\Big(|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u})(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}})\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i,k,l=1,~i\neq l\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[\left|\Big(|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u})X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}\Big)\right|\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\Big(|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u}-X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{u})X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}\Big)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K_{V}}{N}\sum_{i,k,l=1,~i\neq l\neq k}^{N}\bigg(\frac{p-1}{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\frac{1}{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\frac{K_{V}}{N}\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\bigg(\frac{p-1}{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\frac{1}{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]\bigg)

(4.44)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K_{V}(p-1)}{p}\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\frac{K_{V}}{Np}\sum_{i,k,l=1,~i\neq l\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{K_{V}}{N}\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\bigg(\frac{p-1}{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\frac{1}{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]\bigg),

(4.45)

where once again, we split up the summation over
l
and repeatedly apply Assumption
1
and Young’s inequality to obtain (
4.44
) and merely rearranging terms yields (
4.45
).

Case
i\neq j\neq k
:
Summing over all
i,j,k\in\{1,\ldots,N\},~i\neq j\neq k
, we obtain

\displaystyle-\sum_{i,j,k=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\Big(|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u})(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}})\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{i,j,k,l=1,~i\neq j\neq k,~l\neq j\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}-|X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u})(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i,j,k=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\Big(|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u}-X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{u})(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}-X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}})\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i,j,k=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\Big(|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u}-X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{u})(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}-X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}})\Big)\bigg]

(4.46)

\displaystyle\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K_{V}\sum_{i,j,k=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p-1}\cdot\Big(\frac{1}{N}|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|+\frac{1}{N}|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|\Big)\Big]}

(4.47)

\displaystyle\leq K_{V}\sum_{i,j,k=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\bigg(\frac{2(p-1)}{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\frac{1}{pN^{p}}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]\Big)\bigg)

(4.48)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2K_{V}(p-1)}{p}\sum_{i,j,k=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\frac{K_{V}}{pN^{p-1}}\sum_{j,k=1,j\neq k}^{N}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]\Big).

(4.49)

This is once again established via splitting up the sum over
l\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
and using the
symmetrization trick
to obtain (
4.46
) and Assumption
1
to obtain (
4.47
). Young’s inequality yields (
4.48
) before a final rearrangement of terms provides the final inequality (
4.49
).

Part 3: Analysis of the lower order variation term (
4.39
).
Lemma
4.2
with
\lambda_{1}\in(\lambda_{2},\lambda)

implies that there exists
K>0
such that for all
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\},i\neq j,~s\geq t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{i}}|^{p}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{s,x_{i}}|^{p}]\leq Ke^{-\lambda_{1}p(s-t)},

(4.50)

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{j}}|^{p}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N-1}\sum_{k=1,~k\neq j}^{N}\mathbb{E}[|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{s,x_{j}}|^{p}]\leq\frac{K}{N^{p}}e^{-\lambda_{1}p(s-t)},

where for the equality in the second line we used that the starting points
x_{i}
are identically distributed.
For the lower-order variation terms (
4.39
), using Hölder’s inequality, we have that for all
i,j,k\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
and
u\geq t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p-1}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{N}|\partial_{x_{l},x_{l^{\prime}}}^{2}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{u}^{t,\bm{x},N})|~|X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{j}}|~|X^{t,x_{l^{\prime}},l^{\prime},N}_{u,x_{k}}|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{N}|\partial_{x_{l},x_{l^{\prime}}}^{2}B_{i}|_{\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\right]\right)^{(p-1)/p}\cdot\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{j}}|^{p}\ |X^{t,x_{l^{\prime}},l^{\prime},N}_{u,x_{k}}|^{p}\right]\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle=:e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\right]\right)^{(p-1)/p}\cdot I^{1,i,j,k}_{t,u}.

(4.51)

For all
i,j,k\in\{1,\ldots,N\},~u\geq t\geq 0
, we defined the following:

\displaystyle I^{1,i,j,k}_{t,u}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{l=1}^{N}\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{N}|\partial_{x_{l},x_{l^{\prime}}}^{2}B_{i}|_{\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{j}}|^{p}\ |X^{t,x_{l^{\prime}},l^{\prime},N}_{u,x_{k}}|^{p}\right]\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{N}\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{N}|\partial_{x_{l},x_{l^{\prime}}}^{2}B_{i}|_{\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{j}}|^{2p}\right]\ \mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{l^{\prime}},l^{\prime},N}_{u,x_{k}}|^{2p}\right]\right)^{1/2p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\partial_{x_{i},x_{i}}^{2}B_{i}|_{\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j}}|^{2p}\right]\ \mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k}}|^{2p}\right]\right)^{1/2p}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l^{\prime}=1,l^{\prime}\neq i}^{N}|\partial^{2}_{x_{i},x_{l^{\prime}}}B_{i}|_{\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},N}_{u,x_{j}}|^{2p}\right]\ \mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{l^{\prime}},l^{\prime},N}_{u,x_{k}}|^{2p}\right]\right)^{1/2p}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1,l\neq i}^{N}|\partial^{2}_{x_{l},x_{i}}B_{i}|_{\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{l},N}_{u,x_{j}}|^{2p}\right]\ \mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k}}|^{2p}\right]\right)^{1/2p}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1,~i\neq l}^{N}\sum_{l^{\prime}=1,~i\neq l^{\prime}}^{N}|\partial^{2}_{x_{l},x_{l^{\prime}}}B_{i}|_{\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{j}}|^{2p}\right]\ \mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{l^{\prime}},l^{\prime},N}_{u,x_{k}}|^{2p}\right]\right)^{1/2p},

where we implemented the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality in the first inequality. Now using Assumption
2
,

\displaystyle|\partial^{2}_{x_{l},x_{l^{\prime}}}B_{i}|_{\infty}=\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}(1),\quad&i=l=l^{\prime},\\
\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}),\quad&i=l\neq l^{\prime}~\textit{or}~i=l^{\prime}\neq l~\textit{or}~i\neq l=l^{\prime},\\
0,\quad&i\neq l\neq l^{\prime},\end{cases}

and the results in (
4.50
) show that there exists
K>0
such that

\displaystyle I^{1,i,j,k}_{t,u}\leq Ke^{-2\lambda_{1}(u-t)}\cdot\begin{cases}1,\quad&i=j=k,\\
N^{-1},\quad&i=j\neq k~\textit{or}~i=k\neq j~\textit{or}~i\neq j=k,\\
N^{-2},\quad&i\neq j\neq k.\end{cases}

(4.52)

Having established this general estimate for
I^{1,i,j,k}_{t,u}
in (
4.51
), we distinguish the following scenarios:

Case 1:
i=j=k
.
We have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}|^{p}\right]\right)^{(p-1)/p}\cdot I^{1,i,i,i}_{t,u}

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}|^{p}\right]\right)^{(p-1)/p}\cdot e^{-2\lambda_{1}(u-t)}

\displaystyle=Ke^{(\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1})(u-t)}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}|^{p}\right]\right)^{(p-1)/p}e^{(p-1)\lambda_{4}(u-t)}

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{(\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1})(u-t)}\cdot\Big(N+e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}|^{p}\right]\Big),

(4.53)

where we deploy (
4.52
) to obtain the first inequality. Using Young’s inequality
ab\leq a^{q_{1}}/q_{1}+b^{q_{2}}/q_{2}
with
a=\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}|^{p}\right]\right)^{(p-1)/p}e^{(p-1)\lambda_{4}(u-t)}
,
b=1
,
q_{1}=p/(p-1)
,
q_{2}=p
yields (
4.53
).

Case 2:
i\neq j=k
.
We have

\displaystyle\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}|^{p}\right]\right)^{(p-1)/p}\cdot I^{1,i,k,k}_{t,u}

\displaystyle\leq e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}|^{p}\right]\right)^{(p-1)/p}\cdot\frac{K}{N}e^{-2\lambda_{1}(u-t)}

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{(\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1})(u-t)}\cdot\Big(\frac{1}{N^{p-2}}+e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}|^{p}\right]\Big),

(4.54)

where we used Young’s inequality
ab\leq a^{q_{1}}/q_{1}+b^{q_{2}}/q_{2}
with
a=\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}|^{p}\right]\right)^{(p-1)/p}e^{(p-1)\lambda_{1}(u-t)}
,
b=1/N
,
q_{1}=p/(p-1)
,
q_{2}=p
to derive (
4.54
).

Similar calculations apply to the cases
i=j\neq k
and
i=k\neq j
:

\displaystyle\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\right]\right)^{(p-1)/p}\cdot I^{1,i,i,k}_{t,u}

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{(\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1})(u-t)}\cdot\Big(\frac{1}{N^{p-2}}+e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\right]\Big),

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j=1,~i\neq j}^{N}e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{j}}|^{p}\right]\right)^{(p-1)/p}\cdot I^{1,i,j,i}_{t,u}

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{(\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1})(u-t)}\cdot\Big(\frac{1}{N^{p-2}}+e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\sum_{i,k=j,~i\neq j}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{i}}|^{p}\right]\Big).

Case 3:
i\neq j\neq k
.
We have

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,k=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\right]\right)^{(p-1)/p}\cdot I^{1,i,j,k}_{t,u}

\displaystyle\leq e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\sum_{i,j,k=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\right]\right)^{(p-1)/p}\cdot\frac{K}{N^{2}}e^{-2\lambda_{1}(u-t)}

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{(\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1})(u-t)}\cdot\Big(\frac{1}{N^{2p-3}}+e^{p\lambda_{4}(u-t)}\sum_{i,j,k=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\right]\Big),

(4.55)

where we used Young’s inequality
ab\leq a^{q_{1}}/q_{1}+b^{q_{2}}/q_{2}
with
a=(\mathbb{E}[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}])^{(p-1)/p}e^{(p-1)\lambda_{4}(u-t)}
,
b=1/N^{2}
,
q_{1}=p/(p-1)
,
q_{2}=p
to derive (
4.55
).

Part 4: Collecting estimates and conclusion.
Gathering up all the results in (
4.41
)–(
4.55
) and recalling the definition of
I_{t,s}^{2,p}
in (
4.36
), we have for all
\lambda_{4}\in(0,\min\{\lambda-(2+1/N)K_{V},\lambda_{3}\})
,

\displaystyle I_{t,s}^{2,p}

\displaystyle\leq p\int_{t}^{s}\Big(-\lambda+\lambda_{4}+\frac{2K_{V}(p-1)}{p}+Ke^{(\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1})(u-t)}\Big)I_{t,u}^{p}\mathrm{d}u+pK\int_{t}^{s}e^{(\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1})(u-t)}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\quad+p\int_{t}^{s}\frac{K_{V}}{N^{2}p}\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]\mathrm{d}u+p\int_{t}^{s}\frac{K_{V}}{Np}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}|^{p}\Big]\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\quad+p\int_{t}^{s}\bigg(\frac{2K_{V}N^{p-3}}{p}\sum_{i,j,k=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+K_{V}N^{p-3}\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{i}}|^{p}\Big]\Big)~\bigg)~\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\quad+p\int_{t}^{s}\frac{K_{V}}{p}N^{p-2}\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]\Big)\mathrm{d}u.

Combining the terms above, we further have

\displaystyle I_{t,s}^{2,p}

\displaystyle\leq p\int_{t}^{s}\Big(-\lambda+\lambda_{4}+\frac{2K_{V}(p-1)}{p}+Ke^{(\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1})(u-t)}\Big)I_{t,u}^{p}\mathrm{d}u+pK\int_{t}^{s}e^{(\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1})(u-t)}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\quad+p\int_{t}^{s}\frac{K_{V}}{p}\bigg(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{i}}|^{p}\Big]+N^{p-2}\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]\Big)\bigg)\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\quad+p\int_{t}^{s}\frac{K_{V}}{N}\bigg(N^{p-2}\sum_{i,k=1,~i\neq k}^{N}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{i},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{k},x_{i}}|^{p}\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{2}{pN^{p-1}}N^{2p-3}\sum_{i,j,k=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{u,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{p}\Big]\bigg)\mathrm{d}u.

Hence, using
I^{2,p}_{t,s}
in (
4.36
) to dominate the sums of expectations (in each integral) we have

\displaystyle I_{t,s}^{2,p}

\displaystyle\leq p\int_{t}^{s}\Big(-\lambda+\lambda_{4}+2K_{V}+\frac{K_{V}}{N}+Ke^{(\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1})(u-t)}\Big)I_{t,u}^{2,p}\mathrm{d}u+pK\int_{t}^{s}e^{(\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1})(u-t)}\mathrm{d}u.

(4.56)

Recalling that is
\lambda_{4}
chosen such that
\lambda_{4}\in(0,\min\{\lambda-(2+1/N)K_{V},\lambda_{3}\})
and
0<\lambda_{3}<\lambda_{1}<\lambda
, which in turn implies
\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1}<0
,
we have

\displaystyle I_{t,s}^{2,p}

\displaystyle\leq pK\int_{t}^{s}e^{(\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1})(u-t)}I_{t,u}^{2,p}\mathrm{d}u+pK\int_{t}^{s}e^{(\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1})(u-t)}\mathrm{d}u\leq K,

where for the last inequality, we used Gronwall’s inequality (see Lemma
A.6
) with

\displaystyle\alpha(s)=pKe^{(\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1})(s-t)},\quad\beta(s)=pK\int_{t}^{s}e^{(\lambda_{4}-2\lambda_{1})(u-t)}\mathrm{d}u\leq{pK}/({2\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{4}})\quad\textrm{and}

u(s)=I_{t,s}^{2,p}
in combination with the estimate
\int_{t}^{u}\alpha(s)\mathrm{d}s\leq{pK}/({2\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{4}})
.

∎

4.3
The n-Variation process

Let
T\geq s\geq t\geq 0
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
1<n\leq 6
be an integer.
Recalling the quantity
\Pi_{n}^{N}
in Definition
3.1
, the
n
-variation process of
(\bm{X}^{t,\bm{x},N}_{s})_{s\geq t\geq 0}
is given for
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
,
s\geq t
,
\gamma\in\Pi_{n}^{N}
, by

\displaystyle X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{s}\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{N}\partial_{x_{l}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{u}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{1}}}\Big)_{x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n}}}\mathrm{d}u

(4.57)

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{s}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\partial_{x_{l}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{u}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n}}}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,\beta\in\bigcup_{k=0}^{n-1}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
|\alpha|>0,~\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\in\alpha\shuffle\beta\end{subarray}}\int_{t}^{s}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\Big(\partial_{x_{l}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{u}^{t,\bm{x},N})\Big)_{x_{\alpha_{1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{|\alpha|}}}\Big(X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{1}}}\Big)_{x_{\beta_{1}},\dots,x_{\beta_{|\beta|}}}\mathrm{d}u,

where
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{|\alpha|}),~\beta=(\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{|\beta|})
with
\alpha_{i}\in\{1,\dots,N\}
, and
\beta_{i}\in\{1,\dots,N\}
for
i\in\{1,\dots,|\beta|\}
. To clarify the notation, we present the following examples for
n\in\{2,3\}
:

\displaystyle\Big\{

\displaystyle\alpha,\beta\in\bigcup_{k=0}^{1}\Pi_{k}^{N}:|\alpha|>0,~\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\in\alpha\shuffle\beta\Big\}=\Big\{\big\{\alpha=(\gamma_{2}),\beta=\emptyset\big\}\Big\},

when
\gamma=(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})
; for the case
n=3
and setting
\gamma=(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3})

\displaystyle\Big\{

\displaystyle\alpha,\beta\in\bigcup_{k=0}^{2}\Pi_{k}^{N}:|\alpha|>0,~\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\in\alpha\shuffle\beta\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad=\Big\{\big\{\alpha=(\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}),\beta=\emptyset\big\},\big\{\alpha=(\gamma_{2}),\beta=(\gamma_{3})\big\},\big\{\alpha=(\gamma_{3}),\beta=(\gamma_{2})\big\}\Big\}.

The following lemma is a generalization of Lemma
4.2
and Lemma
4.4
, which describes the behaviour of the higher-order variation processes and is needed in the proofs in Section
5
and Section
6
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let the assumptions of Lemma
4.4
hold and let
p\geq 2
be given.
Consider the
n
-variation process with components

(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n+1}}})_{s\geq t\geq 0}

defined by (
4.57
) for
T\geq s\geq t\geq 0
,
n,N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\gamma\in\Pi_{n+1}^{N}
,
1\leq n\leq 6
. Then for each
1\leq n\leq 6
, there exist constants
\lambda_{0}^{(n)}\in(0,\lambda)
and

K_{1},K_{2}>0
(all independent of
s,t,T
and
N
)
such that for any
m\in\{1,\ldots,n+1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{n+1}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n+1}}}|^{p}\Big]\leq\frac{K_{1}}{N^{p(m-1)-m}}e^{-\lambda_{0}^{(n)}p(s-t)}.

In particular, this implies that, for all
\gamma\in\Pi_{n+1}^{N}
, such that
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m,~m\in\{1,\dots,n+1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n+1}}}|^{p}\Big]\leq\frac{K_{2}}{N^{p(m-1)}}e^{-\lambda_{0}^{(n)}p(s-t)}.

Remark 4.3
.

We take
\gamma\in\Pi_{n+1}^{N}
here in Lemma
4.5
: this still corresponds to the highest order of the derivatives remaining as 6. We add the extra index since
\gamma_{1}
corresponds to the index of the starting position, not an index which corresponds to any derivatives being taken.

Remark 4.4
.

As in Lemma
4.2
, where the constant appearing in the bound of the second variation process
\lambda_{4}
was strictly less than
\lambda_{1}
(the constant which arose bounding the first variation process), we choose
\lambda_{0}^{(n)}
such that the sequence
\lambda_{0}^{(i)}
,
i\in\{1,\ldots,6\}
is a strictly decreasing sequence. Note that here, our
\lambda_{0}^{(1)}
and
\lambda_{0}^{(2)}
subsumes our
\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{4}
in the previous proofs respectively.

Remark 4.5
.

For any
\gamma\in\Pi_{n+1}^{N}
with
n\geq 1,~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m,~m\in\{1,\dots,n+1\}
, there are
\mathcal{O}(N^{m})
sequences of indices that have the same pattern as
\gamma
. This means, for the processes
(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n+1}}})
with given
s,t
in Lemma
4.5
,
there are
\mathcal{O}(N^{m})
identical processes in the summation of the first result in Lemma
4.5
, which implies the second inequality. For example, consider
X^{t,x_{1},1,N}_{s,x_{2},x_{3}}
: There are
N(N-1)(N-2)
random variables that are distributed identically to
X^{t,x_{1},1,N}_{s,x_{2},x_{3}}
such as
X^{t,x_{1},1,N}_{s,x_{3},x_{4}},\dots
. Thus, we derive the second inequality by dividing
N^{3}
and adjust the constant
K
.

Proof.

Note that in the following proof, the positive constant
K
is independent of
s,t,T,N
and may change line by line.

Part 1: Preliminary manipulations.

We shall prove this by induction.
The result follows for
n=1
and
n=2
, from Lemma
4.2
and Lemma
4.4
respectively.

Now, suppose that the claim holds for some
n_{1}\in\mathbb{N},~n_{1}<6
, i.e., there exists a sufficiently small constant
\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1})}\in(0,\lambda)
, such that for
m\in\{1,\ldots,n_{1}+1\}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+1}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+1}}}|^{p}\Big]\leq\frac{K}{N^{p(m-1)-m}}e^{-\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1})}p(s-t)}.

(4.58)

We need to prove the statement for
m\in\{1,\ldots,n_{1}+2\}
, i.e., there exists some constant
\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1}+1)}\in(0,\lambda^{(n_{1})})
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}|^{p}\Big]\leq\frac{K}{N^{p(m-1)-m}}e^{-\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1}+1)}p(s-t)}.

We shall use the induction hypothesis (
4.58
) in
Part 2.2
.

From (
4.57
), we have for all
\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N}

\displaystyle X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{t}^{s}\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{N}\partial_{x_{l}}B_{\gamma_{1}}(\bm{X}_{u}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{2}}}\Big)_{x_{\gamma_{3}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{t}^{s}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\partial_{x_{l}}B_{\gamma_{1}}(\bm{X}_{u}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,\beta\in\bigcup_{k=0}^{n_{1}}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
|\alpha|>0,~\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\alpha\shuffle\beta\end{subarray}}\int_{t}^{s}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\Big(\partial_{x_{l}}B_{\gamma_{1}}(\bm{X}_{u}^{t,\bm{x},N})\Big)_{x_{\alpha_{1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{|\alpha|}}}\Big(X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{2}}}\Big)_{x_{\beta_{1}},\dots,x_{\beta_{|\beta|}}}\mathrm{d}u.

An application of Itô’s formula yields for all
s\geq t
,
\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1}+1)}>0
,

\displaystyle e

{}^{p\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1}+1)}(s-t)}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},{\gamma_{1}},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}|^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle=p\int_{t}^{s}e^{p\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1}+1)}(u-t)}\mathbb{E}\bigg[|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},{\gamma_{1}},N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}|^{p-2}\Big(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},{\gamma_{1}},N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{N}\partial_{x_{l}}B_{\gamma_{1}}(\bm{X}_{u}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,\beta\in\bigcup_{k=0}^{n_{1}}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
|\alpha|>0,~\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\alpha\shuffle\beta\end{subarray}}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\Big(\partial_{x_{l}}B_{\gamma_{1}}(\bm{X}_{u}^{t,\bm{x},N})\Big)_{x_{\alpha_{1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{|\alpha|}}}\Big(X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{2}}}\Big)_{x_{\beta_{1}},\dots,x_{\beta_{|\beta|}}}\Big)\bigg]\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\qquad+p\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1}+1)}\int_{t}^{s}e^{p\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1}+1)}(u-t)}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},{\gamma_{1}},N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}|^{p}\Big]\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\leq p\int_{t}^{s}(\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1}+1)}-\lambda)e^{p\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1}+1)}(u-t)}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},{\gamma_{1}},N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}|^{p}\Big]\mathrm{d}u

(4.59)

\displaystyle\qquad-p\int_{t}^{s}e^{p\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1}+1)}(u-t)}\mathbb{E}\bigg[|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},{\gamma_{1}},N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}|^{p-2}\Big(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},{\gamma_{1}},N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},{\gamma_{1}},N}_{u}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u})\ \big(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},{\gamma_{1}},N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\big)\bigg)\bigg]\mathrm{d}u

(4.60)

\displaystyle\qquad-p\int_{t}^{s}e^{p\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1}+1)}(u-t)}\mathbb{E}\bigg[|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},{\gamma_{1}},N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}|^{p-2}\Big(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},{\gamma_{1}},N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,\beta\in\bigcup_{k=0}^{n_{1}}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
|\alpha|>0,~\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\alpha\shuffle\beta\end{subarray}}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\Big(\partial_{x_{l}}B_{\gamma_{1}}(\bm{X}_{u}^{t,\bm{x},N})\Big)_{x_{\alpha_{1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{|\alpha|}}}\Big(X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{u,x_{\gamma_{2}}}\Big)_{x_{\beta_{1}},\dots,x_{\beta_{|\beta|}}}\bigg)\bigg]\mathrm{d}u.

(4.61)

The analysis of the convolution term (
4.60
) and the lower order variation term (
4.61
), mimic that of the proof
of Lemma
4.4
- all arguments are complete generalizations. We present these for clarity.

For a given
p\geq 2
, we define the process
I_{t,s}^{n_{1}+1,p}
(similar to the term
I_{t,s}^{2,p}
from (
4.36
)):

\displaystyle I_{t,s}^{n_{1}+1,p}:=

\displaystyle e^{p\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1}+1)}(s-t)}\sum_{m=1}^{n_{1}+2}\bigg(N^{(m-1)p-m}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}|^{p}\Big]\bigg)

(4.62)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{n_{1}+2}\bigg(N^{(m-1)p-m}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m}\eqref{eq: aux first integral term lambda0-lambda}+\eqref{eq: n-var - convolution }+\eqref{eq: n-var - lower var }\bigg),

which we now work towards bounding
I_{t,s}^{n_{1}+1,p}\leq K
, by separating our analysis into that of the convolution term (
4.60
) and the lower order variation term (
4.61
).

Part 2.1: Analysis of the convolution term (
4.60
).

We highlight the main steps that deal specifically with the convolution term (
4.60
) (similar to (
4.41
)–(
4.49
) in the proof steps for
n=2
). Summing over
\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m
, gives

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\Big(|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{s})(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}})\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=-

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}))=m\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}))=m-1\end{subarray}}\bigg)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\Big(|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{s})(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}})\Big)\bigg].

(4.63)

To continue from (
4.63
), we need to consider
whether or not
\gamma_{1}
is a unique index in
\gamma
, i.e., the two sums appearing in (
4.63
).

Case 1:
\gamma_{1}
is not a unique index in
\gamma
(i.e.,
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}))=m
)
. Note that this case is only meaningful for
m\in\{1,\ldots,n_{1}+1\}
, as
\gamma
has at most
n_{1}+1
different elements (note that
\gamma_{1}
appears at least twice). Further, we remark that
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m
but
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\bigcup(l))\in\{m,m+1\}
, and therefore we need to consider
X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{\cdot}

and
X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{\cdot}
separately
(in a similar fashion to (
4.41
) and (
4.45
)). Hence, we write

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}))=m\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\Big(|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{s})(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}})\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}))=m\end{subarray}}\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1,\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\bigcup(l))=m\end{subarray}}^{N}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1,\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\bigcup(l))=m+1\end{subarray}}^{N}\bigg)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{s})(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}})\Big)\bigg].

(4.64)

We address each of the inner sums over
l
in (
4.64
) separately. Concretely: for the first inner sum, where the summation is over
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\bigcup(l))=m
with
l
taking values in
\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})
(as per the outer summation), we use the
symmetrization trick
to get (
4.65
) and we substitute the upper indices
\gamma_{1}\mapsto l_{1}
and
l\mapsto l_{2}
for added clarity. For the second inner sum, where the summation is over
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\bigcup(l))=m+1
with
l\notin\gamma
, we apply Young’s inequality to get (
4.66
).
We point out that similar arguments were employed in the estimation of the second variation process – refer to (
4.41
) and (
4.45
) for example. We then have,

(
4.64
)

\displaystyle~\leq-\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+1}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m\\
l_{1},l_{2}\in\gamma\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(|X^{t,x_{l_{1}},l_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+1}}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{l_{1}},l_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+1}}}-|X^{t,x_{l_{2}},l_{2},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+1}}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{l_{2}},l_{2},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+1}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{l_{1}},l_{1},N}_{s}-X^{t,x_{l_{2}},l_{2},N}_{s})(X^{t,x_{l_{1}},l_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+1}}}-X^{t,x_{l_{2}},l_{2},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+1}}})\Big)\bigg]

(4.65)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{K_{V}}{N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}))=m\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1,\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\bigcup(l))=m+1\end{subarray}}^{N}\Big(\frac{p-1}{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big|^{p}\Big]+\frac{1}{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big|^{p}\Big]\Big)

(4.66)

\displaystyle~\leq\frac{K_{V}(p-1)}{p}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}))=m\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big|^{p}\Big]+\frac{K_{V}}{Np}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m+1\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}))=m\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big|^{p}\Big],

(4.67)

where (
4.67
) follows from the fact
\nabla^{2}V(x)\geq 0
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
(implying that (
4.65
) is bounded above by zero), and an application of Jensen’s inequality is used to get (
4.66
).
This will provide acceptable weights in (
4.72
) below.

Case 2:

\gamma_{1}
is a unique index in
\gamma
(i.e.,
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m,~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}))=m-1
)
.
Note that this case is only meaningful for
m\in\{2,\ldots,n_{1}+2\}
, as
\gamma
has at least
2
distinct elements (at least one of
\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{n_{1}+2}
has to be different to
\gamma_{1}
). Further, we remark that
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m
but
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\bigcup(l))\in\{m,m-1\}
, and therefore we need to consider
X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{\cdot}

and
X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{\cdot}
separately
(in a similar fashion to (
4.43
) and (
4.49
)). Hence, we write

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}))=m-1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\Big(|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{s})(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}})\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}))=m-1\end{subarray}}\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1,\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\bigcup(l))=m\end{subarray}}^{N}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1,\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\bigcup(l))=m-1\end{subarray}}^{N}\bigg)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}|^{p-2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{s})(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}})\Big)\bigg].

(4.68)

We exploit the
symmetrization trick
for the summation over
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\bigcup(l))=m
where
l
takes values out of
\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})
, and Young’s inequality for the summation over
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\bigcup(l))=m-1
where
l\in(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}))
(check (
4.43
) and (
4.49
) for example). As before, substitute the new upper indices (
4.68
):
\gamma_{1}\mapsto l_{1}
and
l\mapsto l_{2}
to obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{eq: n-var convolution syme2-old}\leq-\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+1}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m-1\\
l_{1},l_{2}\notin\gamma\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(|X^{t,x_{l_{1}},l_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+1}}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{l_{1}},l_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+1}}}-|X^{t,x_{l_{2}},l_{2},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+1}}}|^{p-2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot X^{t,x_{l_{2}},l_{2},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+1}}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{l_{1}},l_{1},N}_{s}-X^{t,x_{l_{2}},l_{2},N}_{s})(X^{t,x_{l_{1}},l_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+1}}}-X^{t,x_{l_{2}},l_{2},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+1}}})\Big)\bigg]

(4.69)

\displaystyle\quad+K_{V}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}))=m-1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1,\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\bigcup(l))=m-1\end{subarray}}^{N}\Big(\frac{p-1}{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big|^{p}\Big]+\frac{1}{pN^{p}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big|^{p}\Big]\Big)

(4.70)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6K_{V}(p-1)}{p}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}))=m-1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big|^{p}\Big]+\frac{6K_{V}}{pN^{p}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m-1\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}))=m-1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big|^{p}\Big],

(4.71)

where we used
\nabla^{2}V(x)\geq 0
to bound (
4.69
) above by 0, then apply Jensen’s inequality to (
4.70
) to match the acceptable weights on
N
in (
4.72
). The constant 6 arises from the fact that there are at most 6 different values for
l\in\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}),m\leq n_{1}+2\leq 7
such that
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\bigcup(l))=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1}))=m-1
.

Combining case 1 and case 2:

We inject the established estimates in (
4.67
) and (
4.71
) into (
4.63
) and consider the summation (
4.62
), (noting the inclusion of the
N^{(m-1)p-m}
term in the summand) to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{n_{1}+2}\Bigg(N^{(m-1)p-m}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\Big(|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}|^{p-2}X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\nabla^{2}V(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{s})(X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}-X^{t,x_{l},l,N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}})\Big)\bigg]\quad\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{6K_{V}(p-1)}{p}+\frac{K_{V}}{Np}+\frac{6K_{V}}{Np}\Big)\sum_{m=1}^{n_{1}+2}\bigg(N^{(m-1)p-m}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big|^{p}\Big]\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq K_{V}\Big(6+\frac{1}{Np}\Big)\sum_{m=1}^{n_{1}+2}\bigg(N^{(m-1)p-m}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|X^{t,x_{\gamma_{1}},\gamma_{1},N}_{s,x_{\gamma_{2}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n_{1}+2}}}\Big|^{p}\Big]\bigg)

(4.72)

\displaystyle=K_{V}\Big(6+\frac{1}{Np}\Big)e^{-\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1}+1)}(s-t)}I_{s,t}^{n_{1}+1,p}.

(4.73)

Part 2.2: Analysis of the lower order variation term (
4.61
).

We provide the following details on the derivatives of the function
B_{\gamma_{1}}
: We notice that, from Assumption
2
, for any
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{l}}B_{\gamma_{1}}=\begin{cases}-\nabla^{2}U(x_{\gamma_{1}})-\frac{1}{N}\partial_{x_{l}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\nabla V(x_{\gamma_{1}}-x_{j})=-\nabla^{2}U(x_{\gamma_{1}})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\nabla^{2}V(x_{\gamma_{1}}-x_{j}),&{\gamma_{1}}=l,\\
-\frac{1}{N}\partial_{x_{l}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\nabla V(x_{\gamma_{1}}-x_{j})=\frac{1}{N}\nabla^{2}V(x_{\gamma_{1}}-x_{l}),&{\gamma_{1}}\neq l.\end{cases}

Thus, we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{x_{l}}B_{\gamma_{1}}|_{\infty}\leq\begin{cases}|\nabla^{2}U|_{\infty}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}|\nabla^{2}V|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(1),&{\gamma_{1}}=l,\\
\frac{1}{N}|\nabla^{2}V|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}),&{\gamma_{1}}\neq l,\end{cases}

(4.74)

and

\displaystyle|\partial^{2}_{x_{l},x_{l^{\prime}}}B_{\gamma_{1}}|_{\infty}\leq\begin{cases}|\nabla^{3}U|_{\infty}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}|\nabla^{3}V|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(1),\quad&{\gamma_{1}}=l=l^{\prime},\\
\frac{1}{N}|\nabla^{3}V|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}),\quad&{\gamma_{1}}=l\neq l^{\prime},\\
\frac{1}{N}|\nabla^{3}V|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}),\quad&{\gamma_{1}}=l^{\prime}\neq l,\\
\frac{1}{N}|\nabla^{3}V|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}),\quad&{\gamma_{1}}\neq l=l^{\prime},\\
0,\quad&{\gamma_{1}}\neq l\neq l^{\prime}.\end{cases}

Using this methodology, one can easily establish that for
n\geq 1,~\eta\in\Pi_{n}^{N}

\displaystyle|\partial^{n}_{x_{\eta_{1}},\dots,x_{\eta_{n}}}B_{\gamma_{1}}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}(N^{1-\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\eta\bigcup(\gamma_{1}))}),\qquad&\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\eta\bigcup(\gamma_{1}))\in\{1,2\},\\
0,\qquad&\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\eta\bigcup(\gamma_{1}))\geq 3,\end{cases}

(4.75)

and further using that only tuples of the form
(\gamma_{1},\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n})
, where elements take at most two different values, yield the non-zero contribution in (
4.75
).

Proceeding exactly as in the manner of (
4.52
)–(
4.55
), applying our induction hypothesis (
4.58
), we can establish the existence of positive constants
K
and
\alpha
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{n_{1}+2}\bigg(N^{(m-1)p-m}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{n_{1}+2}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m}\eqref{eq: n-var - lower var }\bigg)\leq K\int_{t}^{s}e^{-\alpha(u-t)}\mathrm{d}u+K\int_{t}^{s}e^{-\alpha(u-t)}I_{t,u}^{n_{1}+1,p}\mathrm{d}u.

(4.76)

Part 3: Collecting terms and conclusion
.
By collecting (
4.59
), (
4.73
) and (
4.76
), we have that

\displaystyle I_{t,s}^{n_{1}+1,p}\leq

\displaystyle K\int_{t}^{s}e^{-\alpha(u-t)}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle+p\Big(\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1}+1)}-\lambda+K_{V}(6+\frac{1}{Np})\Big)\int_{t}^{s}I_{t,u}^{n_{1}+1,p}\mathrm{d}u+K\int_{t}^{s}e^{-\alpha(u-t)}I_{t,u}^{n_{1}+1,p}\mathrm{d}u.

Using the convexity assumption
\lambda\geq 7K_{V}
, we can conclude the existence of
\lambda_{0}^{(n_{1}+1)}\in(0,\lambda)
such that the term in front of the second integral remains negative. The result follows from Gronwall’s inequality (as was done in the case
n=2
).
∎

5
Decay estimates for the Kolmogorov backward equation

We establish the following estimates for the derivatives of the solution to the Kolmogorov backward equation in terms of moments of the variation processes. This will further help us to apply the results developed in Section
4
to the weak error analysis in Section
6
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
u
be the solution to the Kolmogorov backward equation (
3.4
) with
g
as in Assumption
2
and let
T\geq t\geq 0,N\in\mathbb{N}
. Then there exists a constant
K>0
(independent of
t,T,N
), such that for any
j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
and
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}

\displaystyle|\partial_{x_{j}}u(t,\bm{x})|^{2}\leq\frac{K}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X_{T,x_{j}}^{t,x_{j},j,N}|^{2}\Big]+\frac{K}{N}\sum_{i=1,~i\neq j}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{T,x_{j}}^{t,x_{i},i,N}|^{2}\right].

Proof.

From the definition of
u
, using
[
33
, Theorem 5.5 in Chapter 5]
as in
[
35
, Proposition B.3]
, we deduce

\displaystyle|\partial_{x_{j}}

\displaystyle u(t,\bm{x})|^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[~\sum_{i=1}^{N}\big(\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})\big)\cdot\big(X_{T,x_{j}}^{t,x_{i},i,N}\big)\Big]\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[|\partial_{x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})|\ |X_{T,x_{j}}^{t,x_{j},j,N}|\Big]\Big|^{2}+2\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[~\sum_{i=1,~i\neq j}^{N}\big(\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})\big)\cdot\big(X_{T,x_{j}}^{t,x_{i},i,N}\big)\Big]\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X_{T,x_{j}}^{t,x_{j},j,N}|^{2}\Big]+KN\sum_{i=1,~i\neq j}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\Big||\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})|\ |X_{T,x_{j}}^{t,x_{i},i,N}|\Big|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X_{T,x_{j}}^{t,x_{j},j,N}|^{2}\Big]+\frac{K}{N}\sum_{i=1,~i\neq j}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{T,x_{j}}^{t,x_{i},i,N}|^{2}\right],

where we used Jensen’s inequality and the growth of derivatives of
g
in Assumption
2
.
∎

The next result, Lemma
5.2
, generalizes Lemma
5.1
to derivatives of order
1<n\leq 6
, whose proof is involved and relies on carefully applying Jensen’s inequality.
The next calculations aim to clarify that partitioning summations in particular ways before the application of Jensen’s inequality can yield sharper upper bounds.

For each
\gamma=(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{n})\in\Pi_{n}^{N}
, let
x_{\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{n}}
be a real number. Then applying Jensen’s inequality directly we have

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{1}^{N}}x_{\gamma_{1}}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}\Big|^{2}=N^{2}\Big|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}\Big|^{2}\leq N\sum_{i=1}^{N}|x_{i}|^{2},

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{n}^{N}}x_{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{n}}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle=N^{2n}\Big|\frac{1}{N^{n}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{n}^{N}}x_{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{n}}\Big|^{2}\leq\frac{N^{2n}}{N^{n}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{n}^{N}}|x_{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{n}}|^{2}=N^{n}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{n}^{N}}|x_{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{n}}|^{2}.

(5.1)

Consider now the specific two-dimensional example of
x_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}=N^{1-\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)}
(corresponding to an
N\times N
matrix with diagonal entries
1
and otherwise
1/N
) . Using (
5.1
) we have that

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{2}^{N}}x_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq N^{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}|x_{i,j}|^{2}=N^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|x_{i,i}|^{2}+N^{2}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{N}|x_{i,j}|^{2}=N^{3}+N^{2}\leq 2N^{3}.

This estimate is too naive and can be improved, as we can instead consider

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{2}^{N}}x_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{i,i}\Big|^{2}+2\Big|\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{N}x_{i,j}\Big|^{2}\leq 2N\sum_{i=1}^{N}|x_{i,i}|^{2}+2N^{2}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{N}|x_{i,j}|^{2}

\displaystyle=2N^{2}+\frac{2N^{3}(N-1)}{N^{2}}\leq 4N^{2},

which is indeed a sharper upper bound.

This argument can be extended to the general
n
-dimensional case; that is, take
x_{\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{n}}=N^{1-\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)}
as the entries of a
\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{R}^{N}
-valued
n
-tensor. Observe that

\displaystyle|\{\gamma\in\Pi_{n}^{N}:\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m\}|=\mathcal{O}(N^{m}),

(5.2)

a result which follows from a simple combinatorial argument: regardless of the length
n
of
\gamma
, we have
m
distinct values chosen out of the range
\{1,\ldots,N\}
; that is
\mathcal{O}(N^{m})

possibilities. We have

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{n}^{N}}x_{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{n}}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big|\sum_{m=1}^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m\end{subarray}}x_{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{n}}\Big|^{2}\leq K\sum_{m=1}^{n}\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m\end{subarray}}x_{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{n}}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{m=1}^{n}N^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Pi_{n}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=m\end{subarray}}|x_{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{n}}|^{2}\leq K\sum_{m=1}^{n}N^{2m}N^{2-2m}\leq KN^{2},

with
K
a constant, changing across the inequalities, independent of
N
but dependent on
n
where
n\leq 6
.
This is also a sharper upper bound than applying Jensen’s inequality directly. We now state the generalization of Lemma
5.1
for
1\leq n\leq 6
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
u
satisfy the Kolmogorov backward equation (
3.4
) with
g
as in Assumption
2
and let
T\geq t\geq 0,N\in\mathbb{N}
. Then there exists a constant
K>0
(independent of
t,T,N
), such that for any
n\in\mathbb{N},1\leq n\leq 6
,
\gamma\in\Pi_{n}^{N}
, and

\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}

\displaystyle|\partial

{}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n}}}^{n}u(t,\bm{x})|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{m=0}^{n}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m\end{subarray}}~N^{m-2\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{|\ell|}\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\bigcup_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\alpha_{i}\simeq\gamma\end{subarray}}~\mathbb{E}\Big[~\prod_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\Big|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X_{T,x_{\alpha_{i,1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|}}}^{t,x_{\ell_{i}},\ell_{i},N}}\Big|^{2}~\Big]},

(5.3)

where
\alpha_{i}=(\alpha_{i,1},\dots,\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|})

and
\alpha_{i,j}\in\{1,\dots,N\}
for
j\in\{1,\dots,|\alpha_{i}|\}
.
Further, we have

\displaystyle|\partial

{}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n}}}^{n}u(t,\bm{x})|^{4}

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{m=0}^{n}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m\end{subarray}}~N^{3m-4\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)}~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{|\ell|}\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\bigcup_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\alpha_{i}\simeq\gamma\end{subarray}}~\mathbb{E}\Big[~\prod_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\Big|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X_{T,x_{\alpha_{i,1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|}}}^{t,x_{\ell_{i}},\ell_{i},N}}\Big|^{4}~\Big].

(5.4)

Proof.

Note that in the following proof, the positive constant
K
is independent of
t,T,N
and may change line by line.
Before establishing the main results (
Part 2
and
Part 3
), we present (
Part 1
) a study for the second derivatives of
u
(i.e., for the case
n=2
) to demonstrate the approach used and its nuances in regards to estimate (
5.3
) of the lemma. In the following text, we use
i\neq j\neq k
to denote all three indices are distinct from one another.

Part 1: Comparing the direct calculation with (
5.3
)
for the example case
n=2
. For all
j,k\in\{1,\dots,N\}
,
j\neq k
, using
[
33
, Theorem 5.5 in Chapter 5]
, we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{x_{j},x_{k}}^{2}u(t,\bm{x})|^{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg|\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j},x_{k}}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{N}\partial^{2}_{x_{i},x_{i^{\prime}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{i^{\prime}},{i^{\prime}},N}_{T,x_{k}}\Big]\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K\Bigg(\bigg|\mathbb{E}\Big[\partial_{x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{j},x_{k}}+\partial_{x_{k}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{j},x_{k}}\Big]\bigg|^{2}

(5.5)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg|\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j},x_{k}}~\Big]\bigg|^{2}

(5.6)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg|\mathbb{E}\Big[\partial^{2}_{x_{j},x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{j},{j},N}_{T,x_{k}}+\partial^{2}_{x_{k},x_{k}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{k},{k},N}_{T,x_{k}}\Big]\bigg|^{2}

(5.7)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg|\mathbb{E}\Big[\partial^{2}_{x_{j},x_{k}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{k},{k},N}_{T,x_{k}}+\partial^{2}_{x_{k},x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{j},{j},N}_{T,x_{k}}\Big]\bigg|^{2}

(5.8)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg|\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\partial^{2}_{x_{i},x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{i},{i},N}_{T,x_{k}}\Big]\bigg|^{2}

(5.9)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg|\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\Big(\partial^{2}_{x_{j},x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{i},{i},N}_{T,x_{k}}+\partial^{2}_{x_{i},x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{j},{j},N}_{T,x_{k}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\partial^{2}_{x_{k},x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{i},{i},N}_{T,x_{k}}+\partial^{2}_{x_{i},x_{k}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{k},{k},N}_{T,x_{k}}\Big)\Big]\bigg|^{2}

(5.10)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg|\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i,i^{\prime}=1,~j\neq i\neq i^{\prime}\neq k}^{N}\partial^{2}_{{x_{i},x_{i^{\prime}}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{i^{\prime}},{i^{\prime}},N}_{T,x_{k}}\Big]\bigg|^{2}\bigg),

(5.11)

where the inequality follows simply by separating the summation terms by number of distinct derivatives of
g
matching also to the order of decay in
N
, and then applying Jensen’s inequality (but still leaving the square outside the expectations).

Under Assumption
2
on the derivatives of
g
and observing that (
5.6
), (
5.9
) and (
5.10
) have
\mathcal{O}(N)
terms and that (
5.11
) has
\mathcal{O}(N^{2})
terms in the summand, we apply Jensen’s inequality once more to get

\displaystyle\eqref{eq: lemma5.2 line 3}+\eqref{eq: lemma5.2 line 5}+\eqref{eq: lemma5.2 line 11}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{j},{j},N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{k},{k},N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 89.626pt+\sum_{i,i^{\prime}=1,~j\neq i\neq i^{\prime}\neq k}^{N}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{i^{\prime}},{i^{\prime}},N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\eqref{eq: lemma5.2 line 4}+\eqref{eq: lemma5.2 line 8}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{N}\sum_{i=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{i},{i},N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}\Big]

(
5.8
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{N^{4}}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{k},{k},N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{j},{j},N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}\Big]

(
5.10
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{N^{3}}\sum_{i=1,~i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{i},{i},N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{j},{j},N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 89.626pt+|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}X^{t,x_{k},{k},N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}\Big].

We now demonstrate how these estimates (
5.5
)–(
5.11
), when combined together, can be upper bounded by the form given in (
5.3
) for
n=2
,
\gamma=(j,k),~j\neq k
:

\displaystyle|\partial

{}_{x_{j},x_{k}}^{2}u(t,\bm{x})|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{m=0}^{2}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{2}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m\end{subarray}}~N^{m-2\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{|\ell|}\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{2}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\bigcup_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\alpha_{i}\simeq\gamma\end{subarray}}~\mathbb{E}\Big[~\prod_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\Big|X_{T,x_{\alpha_{i,1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|}}}^{t,x_{\ell_{i}},\ell_{i},N}\Big|^{2}~\Big]}

\displaystyle=K\bigg(\frac{1}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{2}\Big]+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}\Big]+\frac{1}{N^{4}}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{j},k,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1,i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{k},x_{j}}|^{2}\Big]+\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{i=1,i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{j},j,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{k},k,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1,i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,i^{\prime}=1,i\neq j\neq k\\
i\neq i^{\prime},i^{\prime}\neq j\neq k\end{subarray}}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{i^{\prime}},{i^{\prime}},N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{i^{\prime}},{i^{\prime}},N}_{T,x_{j}}|^{2}~|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{k}}|^{2}\Big]\bigg).

(5.12)

Comparing to the results of (
5.5
)–(
5.11
), we can see that (
5.12
) contains more terms. For example, consider the term

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1,i\neq j\neq k}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{2}+|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{k},x_{j}}|^{2}\Big].

For the case
m=1,\ell\in\Pi_{1}^{N}
, we not only take summation over
|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{j},x_{k}}|^{2}
, but also consider
|X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{k},x_{j}}|^{2}
, so that the sum of the terms (
5.5
)–(
5.11
) is bounded above by (
5.12
), verifying our result in the case
n=2
,
\gamma=(j,k),~j\neq k
.

Part 2. The bound (
5.3
).

From the above estimates, one can see that the idea is to essentially partition the sums based on the relationship between indices
i,i^{\prime},j,k
, to keep consistent orders of
N
. Having this separation trick in mind, for any
\gamma\in\Pi_{n}^{N},~1\leq n\leq 6
, we consider (recall the notation for
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\cdot)
and
|\cdot|
in Definition
3.1
):

\displaystyle|\partial^{n}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n}}}u(t,\bm{x})|^{2}

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\Bigg[~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,\beta\in\bigcup_{k=0}^{n-1}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\gamma\setminus(\gamma_{1})\in\alpha\shuffle\beta\end{subarray}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big(\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})\Big)_{x_{\alpha_{1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{|\alpha|}}}\big(X^{t,x_{i},i,N}_{T,x_{\gamma_{1}}}\big)_{x_{\beta_{1}},\dots,x_{\beta_{|\beta|}}}\Bigg]\Bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\Bigg[~\sum_{m=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell\in\Pi_{m}^{N}}\bigg(\Big|\partial^{m}_{{x_{\ell_{1}},\dots,x_{\ell_{m}}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{m}\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}\alpha_{i}\simeq\gamma\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\Big|X_{T,x_{\alpha_{i,1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|}}}^{t,x_{\ell_{i}},\ell_{i},N}\Big|~\bigg)\Bigg]\Bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\Bigg[~\sum_{m=1}^{n}~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)=m\end{subarray}}~\bigg(\Big|\partial^{|\ell|}_{{x_{\ell_{1}},\dots,x_{\ell_{|\ell|}}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{|\ell|}\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\bigcup_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\alpha_{i}\simeq\gamma\end{subarray}}~\prod_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\Big|X_{T,x_{\alpha_{i,1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|}}}^{t,x_{\ell_{i}},\ell_{i},N}\Big|~\bigg)\Bigg]\Bigg|^{2}

(5.13)

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\Bigg[~\sum_{m=0}^{n}~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m\end{subarray}}~\bigg(\Big|\partial^{|\ell|}_{{x_{\ell_{1}},\dots,x_{\ell_{|\ell|}}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{|\ell|}\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\bigcup_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\alpha_{i}\simeq\gamma\end{subarray}}~\prod_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\Big|X_{T,x_{\alpha_{i,1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|}}}^{t,x_{\ell_{i}},\ell_{i},N}\Big|~\bigg)\Bigg]\Bigg|^{2}

(5.14)

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{m=0}^{n}N^{2m}~\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg|\frac{1}{N^{m}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m\end{subarray}}\bigg(\Big|\partial^{|\ell|}_{{x_{\ell_{1}},\dots,x_{\ell_{|\ell|}}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 170.71652pt\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{|\ell|}\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\bigcup_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\alpha_{i}\simeq\gamma\end{subarray}}~\prod_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\Big|X_{T,x_{\alpha_{i,1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|}}}^{t,x_{\ell_{i}},\ell_{i},N}\Big|~\bigg)\Bigg|^{2}~\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{m=0}^{n}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m\end{subarray}}~N^{m}~\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big|\partial^{|\ell|}_{{x_{\ell_{1}},\dots,x_{\ell_{|\ell|}}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})\Big|^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{|\ell|}\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\bigcup_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\alpha_{i}\simeq\gamma\end{subarray}}~\prod_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\Big|X_{T,x_{\alpha_{i,1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|}}}^{t,x_{\ell_{i}},\ell_{i},N}\Big|^{2}~\Bigg]

(5.15)

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{m=0}^{n}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m\end{subarray}}~N^{m-2\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)}~\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{|\ell|}\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\bigcup_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\alpha_{i}\simeq\gamma\end{subarray}}~\prod_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\Big|X_{T,x_{\alpha_{i,1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|}}}^{t,x_{\ell_{i}},\ell_{i},N}\Big|^{2}~\Bigg],

where in (
5.13
) and (
5.14
), we regroup the summation over all
\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N}
based on the magnitude of
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)
and
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)
.
In (
5.15
), we apply Jensen’s inequality to the second summation where the set
\{\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N}:\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m\}
has
\mathcal{O}(N^{m})
terms (
\ell
has
m
degrees of freedom), thus we end up with a factor of
N^{m}
after calculation. For the last line, we used Assumption
2
:
\big|\partial^{|\ell|}_{x_{\ell_{1}},\dots,x_{\ell_{|\ell|}}}g\big|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(N^{-\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)})
.

Part 3: The bound (
5.4
).

One proves (
5.4
) using the same arguments as in
Part 2
. We have

\displaystyle|\partial^{n}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n}}}u(t,\bm{x})|^{4}

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{m=0}^{n}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m\end{subarray}}~N^{3m}~\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big|\partial^{|\ell|}_{{x_{\ell_{1}},\dots,x_{\ell_{|\ell|}}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t,\bm{x},N})\Big|^{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{|\ell|}\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\bigcup_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\alpha_{i}\simeq\gamma\end{subarray}}~\prod_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\Big|X_{T,x_{\alpha_{i,1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|}}}^{t,x_{\ell_{i}},\ell_{i},N}\Big|^{4}~\Bigg]

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq K\sum_{m=0}^{n}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m\end{subarray}}~N^{3m-4\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)}~\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{|\ell|}\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\bigcup_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\alpha_{i}\simeq\gamma\end{subarray}}~\prod_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\Big|X_{T,x_{\alpha_{i,1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|}}}^{t,x_{\ell_{i}},\ell_{i},N}\Big|^{4}~\Bigg].}

∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
u
satisfy the Kolmogorov backward equation (
3.4
) with
g
as in Assumption
2
,
let
T\geq t\geq 0,N\in\mathbb{N}
and assume that the starting points
x_{i}
are
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable random variables in
L^{2}(\Omega,\mathbb{R})
sampled from the same distribution for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.
Then there exist positive constants
K
,
\lambda_{0}\in(0,\lambda)
(both are independent of
t,T,N
) such that for any
1\leq n\leq 6
,
\gamma\in\Pi_{n}^{N}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\partial^{n}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n}}}u(t,\bm{x})\big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{-\lambda_{0}(T-t)}N^{-2\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)},

(5.16)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\partial^{n}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n}}}u(t,\bm{x})\big|^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{-\lambda_{0}(T-t)}N^{-4\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)}.

(5.17)

Proof.

Note that in the following proof, the positive constants
K,\lambda_{0}
are independent of
t,T,N
and may change line by line.
Recall the results and notations in Lemma
5.2
, after taking the expectation, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\partial

{}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n}}}^{n}u(t,\bm{x})\big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{m=0}^{n}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m\end{subarray}}~N^{m-2\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)}~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{|\ell|}\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\bigcup_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\alpha_{i}\simeq\gamma\end{subarray}}~\mathbb{E}\Big[~\prod_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\Big|X_{T,x_{\alpha_{i,1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|}}}^{t,x_{\ell_{i}},\ell_{i},N}\Big|^{2}~\Big]

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{m=0}^{n}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m\end{subarray}}~N^{m-2\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)}~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{|\ell|}\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\bigcup_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\alpha_{i}\simeq\gamma\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[~\Big|X_{T,x_{\alpha_{i,1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|}}}^{t,x_{\ell_{i}},\ell_{i},N}\Big|^{2|\ell|}~\Big]\Big)^{1/|\ell|},

where we employed Hölder’s inequality. We have the following estimate for the variation processes in the above product:

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[~\Big|X_{T,x_{\alpha_{i,1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|}}}^{t,x_{\ell_{i}},\ell_{i},N}\Big|^{2|\ell|}~\Big]\Big)^{1/|\ell|}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{K}{N^{\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\ell_{i})\bigcup\alpha_{i})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\Pi_{|\alpha_{i}|+1}^{N},\\
~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\beta)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\ell_{i})\bigcup\alpha_{i})\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Big[~\Big|X^{t,x_{\beta_{1}},\beta_{1},N}_{T,x_{\beta_{2}},\dots,x_{\beta_{|\beta|}}}\Big|^{2|\ell|}\Big]\bigg)^{1/|\ell|}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{K}{N^{2|\ell|(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\ell_{i})\bigcup\alpha_{i})-1)}}e^{-\lambda_{0}2|\ell|(T-t)}\Big)^{1/|\ell|}

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{-2\lambda_{0}(T-t)}N^{-2(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\ell_{i})\bigcup\alpha_{i})-1)},

where we used the second and first part of Lemma
4.5
with
p=2|\ell|
,
m=\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\ell_{i})\bigcup\alpha_{i})
to obtain the first two inequalities. The summation in the first inequality contains
\mathcal{O}(N^{\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\ell_{i})\bigcup\alpha_{i})})
terms that are identically distributed to the left-hand side process, and it also contains more terms.

4
4

4

For example, the process
X_{T,x_{\alpha_{i,1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|}}}^{t,x_{\ell_{i}},\ell_{i},N}

with
\ell_{i}=1,\alpha_{i}=(1,2,3)
is identically distributed to the process with
\ell_{i}=2,\alpha_{i}=(2,3,4)
but not necessarily identically distributed to the process with
\ell_{i}=3,\alpha_{i}=(4,2,2)
, though they both satisfy
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell_{i}\cup\alpha_{i})=3
due to the different derivative patterns.

We now relate the orders
(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\ell_{i})\bigcup\alpha_{i})-1)
from the previous estimate and
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)
appearing in
(
5.16
), by first showing that

\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus\ell)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\Big(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\ell_{i})\bigcup\alpha_{i})-1\Big)
. Concretely, the constraint in the second summation,
\bigcup_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\alpha_{i}\simeq\gamma
