, implies

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\Big(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\ell_{i})\bigcup\alpha_{i})-1\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\bigg(\hat{\mathcal{O}}\Big((\ell_{i})\bigcup\Big(\alpha_{i}\bigcap(\gamma\setminus\ell)\Big)\bigcup\Big(\alpha_{i}\setminus(\gamma\setminus\ell)\Big)\Big)-1\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\bigg(\hat{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\alpha_{i}\bigcap(\gamma\setminus\ell)\Big)+\hat{\mathcal{O}}\Big((\ell_{i})\bigcup\Big(\alpha_{i}\setminus(\gamma\setminus\ell)\Big)\Big)-1\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\hat{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\alpha_{i}\bigcap(\gamma\setminus\ell)\Big)\geq\hat{\mathcal{O}}\Big((\bigcup_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\alpha_{i})\bigcap(\gamma\setminus\ell)\Big)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus\ell).

Then using the constraint
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m
of the first summation we infer

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\Big(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\ell_{i})\bigcup\alpha_{i})-1\Big)\geq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma\setminus\ell)\geq\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)-\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)}=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m-\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell).

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[|\partial

{}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n}}}^{n}u(t,\bm{x})|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{-2\lambda_{0}(T-t)}\sum_{m=0}^{n}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m\end{subarray}}~N^{m-2\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)}~N^{-2\sum_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\big(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\ell_{i})\bigcup\alpha_{i})-1\big)}

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{-2\lambda_{0}(T-t)}\sum_{m=0}^{n}N^{2m-2\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)-2\big(\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)-\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)+m\big)}\leq Ke^{-2\lambda_{0}(T-t)}N^{-2\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)},

which yields (
5.16
), as sought. Similar calculations deliver (
5.17
). That is, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[|\partial

{}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{n}}}^{n}u(t,\bm{x})|^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{m=0}^{n}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m\end{subarray}}~N^{3m-4\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)}~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{|\ell|}\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\bigcup_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\alpha_{i}\simeq\gamma\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[~\Big|X_{T,x_{\alpha_{i,1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{i,|\alpha_{i}|}}}^{t,x_{\ell_{i}},\ell_{i},N}\Big|^{4|\ell|}~\Big]\Big)^{1/|\ell|}

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{-2\lambda_{0}(T-t)}\sum_{m=0}^{n}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\Pi_{k}^{N},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell\bigcup\gamma)=\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)+m\end{subarray}}~N^{3m-4\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)}~N^{-4\sum_{i=1}^{|\ell|}\big(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\ell_{i})\bigcup\alpha_{i})-1\big)}

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{-2\lambda_{0}(T-t)}\sum_{m=0}^{n}N^{4m-4\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)-4\big(\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)-\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\ell)+m\big)}\leq Ke^{-2\lambda_{0}(T-t)}N^{-4\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)}.

∎

6
Weak error expansion and its analysis

For the convenience of the reader, we recall Lemma
3.2
which provides an expansion for the global weak error (
3.1
) under Assumption
2
, for the processes defined in (
2.4
) and (
2.6
) as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[g(\bm{X}^{N}_{T})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\Big[g(\bm{X}^{N,h}_{T})\Big]

\displaystyle=h^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}L(t_{m},\bm{X}^{N,h}_{t_{m}})\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}R(t_{m},\bm{X}^{N,h}_{t_{m}})\right],

(6.1)

where the map
L:\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}\to\mathbb{R}
is defined via the maps
u
and
(B_{i})_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}
:

\displaystyle L(t,\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big[\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}B_{j}(\bm{x})\partial_{x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{x})\partial_{x_{i}}u(t,\bm{x})+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\partial_{x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{x})\partial_{x_{i},x_{j}}^{2}u(t,\bm{x})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\partial^{2}_{x_{j},x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{x})\partial_{x_{i}}u(t,\bm{x})\Big].

(6.2)

The remainder term
R(\cdot,\cdot)
will later be written as a linear combination of 8 remainder terms, which we will analyze in Section
6.2
.

6.1
Estimates for the leading error term
L

We consider the first term in (
6.1
) expressed as a telescoping sum:

\displaystyle h^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}L(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})\Big]

\displaystyle=h\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}L(s,\bm{X}_{s}^{N})\mathrm{d}s\Big]

(6.3)

\displaystyle\qquad+h\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m+1}}\Big(L(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})-L(s,\bm{X}_{s}^{N})\Big)\mathrm{d}s\Big],

(6.4)

and derive the following result.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let Assumption
2
hold and let
\xi\in L^{4}(\Omega,\mathbb{R})
.
Let
L
be defined in (
6.2
). Then there exists a positive constant
\lambda_{0}\in(0,\lambda)
such that

\displaystyle h^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}L(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})\Big]\leq Kh^{3/2}+Khe^{-\lambda_{0}T}.

(6.5)

Proof.

Note that in the following proof, the positive constants
K,\lambda_{0}
are independent of
t,T,N
and may change line by line. The proof is carried out by analysing (
6.3
) and (
6.4
) separately to reach (
6.5
).

Part 1: Estimating (
6.3
).

Let
(\overline{X}_{0}^{1,N},\ldots,\overline{X}_{0}^{N,N})=\overline{\bm{X}}_{0}^{N}\sim\mu^{N,*}
, where
\mu^{N,*}
is the stationary distribution of the IPS (viewed as a
\mathbb{R}^{N}
-valued SDE).

The next result is shown in Lemma (
A.8
) for the benefit of the reader and it follows using integration by parts – the argument can be found in
[
43
, Equation (3.29)]
and leverages the known closed form (up to a scaling constant) of the invariant density map
\mu^{N,*}
(
6.6
) and the definition of the operator
L
in (
6.2
).

For any
t\geq 0
, we then have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[L(t,\overline{\bm{X}}_{t}^{N})\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[L(0,\overline{\bm{X}}_{0}^{N})\Big]=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}L(0,\bm{x})\mu^{N,*}(\mathrm{d}\bm{x})=0,

where we recall the density
\mu^{N,*}
for the particle system (that corresponds to (
1.2
))

\displaystyle\mu^{N,*}(\bm{x})\propto\exp\Big(-\frac{2}{\sigma^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}U(x_{i})-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}V(x_{i}-x_{j})\Big).

(6.6)

We can now start the proof of our result. We may then write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}L(s,\bm{X}_{s}^{N})\mathrm{d}s\Big]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\Big[L(s,\bm{X}_{s}^{N})-L(s,\overline{\bm{X}}_{s}^{N})\Big]\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\langle\partial_{\bm{x}}L\big(s,\rho\bm{X}_{s}^{N}+(1-\rho)\overline{\bm{X}}_{s}^{N}\big),~\bm{X}_{s}^{N}-\overline{\bm{X}}_{s}^{N}\big\rangle\Big]}\mathrm{d}\rho~\mathrm{d}s.

Let
\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N}\coloneqq\rho\bm{X}_{s}^{N}+(1-\rho)\overline{\bm{X}}_{s}^{N}
. Using the chain rule, we deduce the following:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[

\displaystyle\Big\langle\partial_{\bm{x}}L(s,\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N}),~\bm{X}_{s}^{N}-\overline{\bm{X}}_{s}^{N}\Big\rangle\Big]=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{N}\Big(\partial_{x_{k}}B_{j}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial_{x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial_{x_{i}}u(s,\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+B_{j}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial^{2}_{x_{j},x_{k}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial_{x_{i}}u(s,\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})+B_{j}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial_{x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial_{x_{i},x_{k}}^{2}u(s,\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\Big)\cdot\Big(X_{s}^{k,N}-\overline{X}_{s}^{k,N}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{N}\Big(\partial^{2}_{x_{j},x_{k}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial^{2}_{x_{i},x_{j}}u({s},\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})+\partial_{x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial^{3}_{x_{i},x_{j},x_{k}}u({s},\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\Big)\cdot\Big(X_{s}^{k,N}-\overline{X}_{s}^{k,N}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{N}\Big(\partial^{3}_{x_{j},x_{j},x_{k}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial_{x_{i}}u({s},\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})+\partial^{2}_{x_{j},x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial^{2}_{x_{i},x_{k}}u({s},\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\Big)\cdot\Big(X_{s}^{k,N}-\overline{X}_{s}^{k,N}\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{N}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|X_{s}^{k,N}-\overline{X}_{s}^{k,N}\big|^{2}\Big]}\cdot\bigg\{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\partial_{x_{k}}B_{j}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial_{x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial_{x_{i}}u(s,\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\big|^{2}\Big]}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|B_{j}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial^{2}_{x_{j},x_{k}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial_{x_{i}}u(s,\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\big|^{2}\Big]}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|B_{j}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial_{x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial^{2}_{x_{i},x_{k}}u(s,\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\big|^{2}\Big]}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\partial^{2}_{x_{j},x_{k}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial_{x_{i},x_{j}}^{2}u({s},\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\big|^{2}\Big]}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\partial_{x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial_{x_{i},x_{j},x_{k}}^{3}u({s},\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\big|^{2}\Big]}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\partial^{3}_{x_{j},x_{j},x_{k}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial_{x_{i}}u({s},\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\big|^{2}\Big]}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\partial^{2}_{x_{j},x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\partial_{x_{i},x_{k}}^{2}u({s},\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N})\big|^{2}\Big]}~\bigg\},

(6.7)

where we used the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality.
We now work through (
6.7
).
As for the
L^{2}
-distance to the invariant distribution (the first expectation in the sum), note that (
A.11
) in Lemma
A.4
implies that for any
s\geq 0
we have
\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}^{k,N}-\overline{X}_{s}^{k,N}|^{2}]\leq Ke^{-2\lambda s}
.
The approach to deal with the remaining seven terms is more or less identical. We inject the estimate (
5.16
) of Lemma
5.3
for the derivatives of
u
and the bounds for the derivatives of
B
established in (
4.75
). The inequality below preserves the exact ordering of (
6.7
), and we highlight that obtaining (
6.8
) requires the additional use of the linear growth of
B_{j}
, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, the
L^{4}
-estimates of
\bm{X}^{N}
(in Proposition
2.1
) and the
L^{4}
-estimate (
5.17
) for the derivatives of
u
(recalling that
\xi\in L^{4}(\Omega,\mathbb{R})
); for some positive constant
\lambda_{0}\in(0,\lambda)
chosen small enough we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[

\displaystyle\Big\langle\partial_{\bm{x}}L(s,\bm{X}_{s,\rho}^{N}),~\bm{X}_{s}^{N}-\overline{\bm{X}}_{s}^{N}\Big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle\leq K\sqrt{e^{-2\lambda s}}\cdot\sqrt{e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-s)}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi^{N}_{3}}\Big(\frac{1}{N^{(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}))-1)+(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}))-1)+1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\frac{1}{N^{(\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)-1)+1}}+\frac{1}{N^{(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}))-1)+\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\gamma_{1},\gamma_{3}))}}

(6.8)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\frac{1}{N^{(\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)-1)+\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}))}}+\frac{1}{N^{(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}))-1)+\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\frac{1}{N^{(\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)-1)+1}}+\frac{1}{N^{(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}))-1)+\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\gamma_{1},\gamma_{3}))}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq K\sqrt{e^{-2\lambda s}e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-s)}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi^{N}_{3}}\frac{1}{N^{\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)}}

(6.9)

\displaystyle\leq K\sqrt{e^{-2\lambda s}e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-s)}},

(6.10)

where the inequality in (
6.9
) follows from the fact that
\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}))+\hat{\mathcal{O}}((\gamma_{1},\gamma_{3}))-1\geq\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)
for any
\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}
(seen by checking the cases).
The final result (
6.10
) follows from recalling (
5.2
), in turn implying that the summation term in (
6.9
) is indeed
\mathcal{O}(1)
.

To conclude this first part of the proof, we gather our estimates and obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}L(s,\bm{X}_{s}^{N})\mathrm{d}s\Big]

\displaystyle\leq K\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-s)}e^{-2\lambda s}}~\mathrm{d}\rho\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=Ke^{-\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2}T}\int_{0}^{T}e^{\frac{\lambda_{0}-2\lambda}{2}s}\mathrm{d}s\leq Ke^{-\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2}T}.

Part 2: Estimating (
6.4
).

For the term (
6.4
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m+1}}\Big(L(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})

\displaystyle-L(s,\bm{X}_{s}^{N})\Big)\mathrm{d}s\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\Big[L(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})-L(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big]\mathrm{d}s

(6.11)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\Big[L(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})-L(s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big]\mathrm{d}s

(6.12)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\Big[L(s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})-L(s,\bm{X}_{s}^{N})\Big]\mathrm{d}s.

(6.13)

Part 2.1: Estimating (
6.11
) and (
6.13
).

For (
6.11
), similar to the calculations (
6.7
)–(
6.10
) (in the previous part of the proof), we derive

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[

\displaystyle L(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})-L(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\langle\partial_{\bm{x}}L\big(t_{m},\rho\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h}+(1-\rho)\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N}\big),~\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h}-\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N}\Big\rangle\Big]\mathrm{d}\rho

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}~\sqrt{e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-t_{m})}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|X_{t_{m}}^{i,N,h}-X_{t_{m}}^{i,N}\big|^{2}\Big]~}\leq Kh^{1/2}e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-t_{m+1})/2},

(6.14)

where we used Proposition
2.2
for the strong error rate. Similarly, for (
6.13
), we have for
s\in[t_{m},t_{m+1}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[L(s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})-L(s,\bm{X}_{s}^{N})\Big]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\langle\partial_{\bm{x}}L\big(s,\rho\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N}+(1-\rho)\bm{X}_{s}^{N}\big),~\bm{X}_{s}^{N}-\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N}\Big\rangle\Big]\mathrm{d}\rho

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}~\sqrt{e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-s)}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|X_{s}^{i,N}-X_{t_{m}}^{i,N}\big|^{2}\Big]~}

\displaystyle\leq K\sqrt{s-t_{m}}e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-s)/2}\leq Kh^{1/2}e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-t_{m+1})/2},

(6.15)

where we used Proposition
A.1
and that
h\geq s-t_{m}
.

Part 2.2: Estimating (
6.12
).

For the term (
6.12
), we get for all
m
and
s\in[t_{m},t_{m+1}]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[

\displaystyle L(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})-L(s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\bigg(B_{j}(\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\partial_{x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big(\partial_{x_{i}}u(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})-\partial_{x_{i}}u(s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\partial_{x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big(\partial_{x_{i},x_{j}}^{2}u(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})-\partial^{2}_{x_{i},x_{j}}u(s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\partial^{2}_{x_{j},x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big(\partial_{x_{i}}u(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})-\partial_{x_{i}}u(s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|B_{j}(\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\partial_{x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big|^{2}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\partial_{x_{i}}u(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})-\partial_{x_{i}}u(s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big|^{2}\Big]}

(6.16)

\displaystyle\qquad+K\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\partial_{x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big|^{2}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\partial_{x_{i},x_{j}}^{2}u(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})-\partial^{2}_{x_{i},x_{j}}u(s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big|^{2}\Big]}

(6.17)

\displaystyle\qquad+K\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\partial^{2}_{x_{j},x_{j}}B_{i}(\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big|^{2}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\partial_{x_{i}}u(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})-\partial_{x_{i}}u(s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big|^{2}\Big]}.

(6.18)

We first study the differences of first-order derivatives of
u
in (
6.16
) and (
6.18
), and then study the difference of second-order derivatives of
u
(
6.17
). Collecting these estimates and using the bounds on the derivatives of
B
yields the upper bound on (
6.12
). Then we will be in a position to conclude the main result.

Part 2.2.1: The first order derivative terms.
We first derive the following estimate:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{x_{i}}u(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})-\partial_{x_{i}}u(s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big|~~\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[\partial_{x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t_{m},\bm{x},N})\cdot X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},x_{j},j,N}-\partial_{x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{x},N})\cdot X_{T,x_{i}}^{s,x_{j},j,N}\Big]~\bigg|_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{x}=\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N}\end{subarray}}~\Big|^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq K\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\Big|~\mathbb{E}\Big[~\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{N}\Big(\partial_{x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t_{m},\bm{x},N})\cdot X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},x_{j},j,N}-\partial_{x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{x},N})\cdot X_{T,x_{i}}^{s,x_{j},j,N}\Big)\Big]~\bigg|_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{x}=\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N}\end{subarray}}~\Big|^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+K\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\Big|~\mathbb{E}\Big[\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t_{m},\bm{x},N})\cdot X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},x_{i},i,N}-\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{x},N})\cdot X_{T,x_{i}}^{s,x_{i},i,N}\Big]~\bigg|_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{x}=\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N}\end{subarray}}~\Big|^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq K\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\Big|~\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{N}\Big(\partial_{x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\cdot X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{j,N},j,N}-\partial_{x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\cdot X_{T,x_{i}}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{j,N},j,N}\Big)~\Big|^{2}~\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+K\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\Big|~\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\cdot X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{i,N},i,N}-\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\cdot X_{T,x_{i}}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{i,N},i,N}~\Big|^{2}~\bigg]

(6.19)

\displaystyle\leq KN\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{N}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\Big|~\Big(\partial_{x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})-\partial_{x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\Big)\cdot X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{j,N},j,N}\Big|^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+KN\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{N}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\Big|\partial_{x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\cdot\Big(X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{j,N},j,N}-X_{T,x_{i}}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{j,N},j,N}\Big)~\Big|^{2}~\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+K\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\Big|~\Big(\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})-\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\Big)\cdot X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{i,N},i,N}~\Big|^{2}~\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+K\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\Big|~\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\cdot\Big(X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{i,N},i,N}-X_{T,x_{i}}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{i,N},i,N}\Big)~\Big|^{2}~\bigg],

(6.20)

where we employed the Cauchy–Schwarz and Jensen inequalities, as well as the tower property for conditional expectations to obtain (
6.19
). Inequality (
6.20
) follows from a standard re-arrangement of (
6.19
).

An application of Hölder’s inequality and Assumption
2
on the function
g
further yields

(
6.20
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{N}\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{N}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|X_{T}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{j,N},j,N}-X_{T}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{j,N},j,N}\big|^{4}\Big]~\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{j,N},j,N}\big|^{4}\Big]}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{K}{N}\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{j,N},j,N}-X_{T,x_{i}}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{j,N},j,N}\big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{K}{N^{2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|X_{T}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{i,N},i,N}-X_{T}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{i,N},i,N}\big|^{4}\Big]~\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{i,N},i,N}\big|^{4}\Big]}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{K}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{i,N},i,N}-X_{T,x_{i}}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{i,N},i,N}\big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{N^{2}}(s-t_{m})e^{-2\lambda_{2}(T-s)},

(6.21)

where we used
|\partial^{|\gamma|}_{{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{|\gamma|}}}}g|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(N^{-\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)})
(a consequence of Assumption
2
),

and we used Lemma
4.5
with
n=1
(note that
\lambda_{0}
can be replaced by
\lambda_{1}
in this case), Lemma
A.2
and Proposition
4.3
with
\lambda_{2}\in(0,\min\{\lambda-2K_{V},\lambda_{1}\})
for the final inequality.

Part 2.2.2: The second order derivative terms.

Similarly, for the differences of the second order derivatives of
u
in (
6.17
), applying the tower property of the conditional expectation once more we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\Big[\big|\partial_{x_{i},x_{j}}^{2}u(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})-\partial_{x_{i},x_{j}}^{2}u(s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|~~\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[\partial_{x_{k}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t_{m},\bm{x},N})\cdot X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{t_{m},x_{k},k,N}-\partial_{x_{k}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{x},N})\cdot X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{s,x_{k},k,N}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[\partial_{x_{k},x_{k^{\prime}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t_{m},\bm{x},N})\cdot X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},x_{k},k,N}X_{T,x_{j}}^{t_{m},x_{k^{\prime}},k^{\prime},N}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-\partial_{x_{k},x_{k^{\prime}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{x},N})\cdot X_{T,x_{i}}^{s,x_{k},k,N}X_{T,x_{j}}^{s,x_{k^{\prime}},k^{\prime},N}\Big]~\bigg|_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{x}=\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N}\end{subarray}}~\bigg|^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq K\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|~~\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(\partial_{x_{k}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})-\partial_{x_{k}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\Big)\cdot X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},k,N}\Big|^{2}\Big]

(6.22)

\displaystyle\qquad+K\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},k,N}-X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},k,N}\Big)\cdot\partial_{x_{k}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\Big|^{2}\Big]

(6.23)

\displaystyle\qquad+K\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|~~\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=1}^{N}\Big(\partial_{x_{k},x_{k^{\prime}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})-\partial_{x_{k},x_{k^{\prime}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\Big)\cdot X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},k,N}X_{T,x_{j}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{k^{\prime},N},k^{\prime},N}\Big|^{2}\Big]

(6.24)

\displaystyle\qquad+K\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|~~\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=1}^{N}\Big(X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},k,N}-X_{T,x_{i}}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},k,N}\Big)\cdot\partial_{x_{k},x_{k^{\prime}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\cdot X_{T,x_{j}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{k^{\prime},N},k^{\prime},N}\Big|^{2}\Big]

(6.25)

\displaystyle\qquad+K\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|~~\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=1}^{N}\Big(X_{T,x_{j}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{k^{\prime},N},k^{\prime},N}-X_{T,x_{j}}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{k^{\prime},N},k^{\prime},N}\Big)\cdot\partial_{x_{k},x_{k^{\prime}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\cdot X_{T,x_{i}}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},k,N}\Big|^{2}\Big].

(6.26)

We are required to analyze each of the terms (
6.22
)–(
6.26
) separately. By further applying Jensen’s inequality and Hölder’s inequality, we derive the following estimates for the first two terms (
6.22
) and (
6.23
) based on the values of
\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k))

(
6.22
)

\displaystyle\leq K\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|~\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})-\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\Big|^{4}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{i,N},i,N}\Big|^{4}~\Big]}

\displaystyle\qquad+K\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|~\partial_{x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})-\partial_{x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\Big|^{4}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{j,N},j,N}\Big|^{4}~\Big]}

\displaystyle\qquad+NK\sum_{k=1,k\notin\{i,j\}}^{N}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|~\partial_{x_{k}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})-\partial_{x_{k}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\Big|^{4}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},k,N}\Big|^{4}~\Big]}.

(6.27)

Now, similar to (
6.21
), an application of Assumption
2
for the function
g
and then injecting the bounds from Lemma
4.5
with
n=2
(note that
\lambda_{0}
can be replaced by
\lambda_{4}
in this case) and Lemma
A.2
yield

(
6.27
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{N^{2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|X_{T}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{i,N},i,N}-X_{T}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{i,N},i,N}\big|^{4}\Big]~\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{i,N},i,N}\big|^{4}\Big]}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{K}{N^{2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|X_{T}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{j,N},j,N}-X_{T}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{j,N},j,N}\big|^{4}\Big]~\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{j,N},j,N}\big|^{4}\Big]}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{K}{N}\sum_{k=1,k\notin\{i,j\}}^{N}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|X_{T}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},k,N}-X_{T}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},k,N}\big|^{4}\Big]~\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},k,N}\big|^{4}\Big]}

\displaystyle\leq K(s-t_{m})e^{-2\lambda_{2}(T-s)}e^{-2\lambda_{4}(T-t_{m})}\bigg(\frac{1}{N^{2}\cdot N^{2\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))-2}}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1,k\notin\{i,j\}}^{N}\frac{1}{N^{2\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k))-2}}\bigg).

(6.28)

Similarly, for (
6.23
), using Proposition
A.3
, we have

(
6.23
)

\displaystyle\leq K\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\Big(X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{i,N},i,N}-X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{i,N},i,N}\Big)\Big|^{4}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\Big|^{4}\Big]}

\displaystyle\quad+K\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\Big(X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{j,N},j,N}-X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{j,N},j,N}\Big)\Big|^{4}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\partial_{x_{j}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\Big|^{4}\Big]}

\displaystyle\quad+NK\sum_{k=1,k\notin\{i,j\}}^{N}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\Big(X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},k,N}-X_{T,x_{i},x_{j}}^{s,X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},k,N}\Big)\Big|^{4}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\partial_{x_{k}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\Big|^{4}\Big]}

\displaystyle\leq K(s-t_{m})e^{-2\lambda_{4}(T-t_{m})}\bigg(\frac{1}{N^{2}\cdot N^{2\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))-2}}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1,k\notin\{i,j\}}^{N}\frac{1}{N^{2\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k))-2}}\bigg).

(6.29)

For (
6.24
)–(
6.26
), we will make use of the following bound, which follows from exploiting the Lipschitz property of
\partial_{x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime}}g
from Assumption
2
and applying Lemma
A.2

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\partial_{x_{k},x_{k^{\prime}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})-\partial_{x_{k},x_{k^{\prime}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\Big|^{4}\Big]\leq\frac{K}{N^{4\hat{\mathcal{O}}((k,k^{\prime}))}}e^{-4\lambda_{2}(T-s)}.

Hence, similarly to (
6.28
)–(
6.29
), partitioning the sum based on values taken by
\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k,k^{\prime}))

(
6.24
)

\displaystyle\leq K\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,k^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,N\},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k,k^{\prime}))=\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))\end{subarray}}+N\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,k^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,N\},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k,k^{\prime}))=\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))+1\end{subarray}}+N^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,k^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,N\},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k,k^{\prime}))=\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))+2\end{subarray}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\partial_{x_{k},x_{k^{\prime}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})-\partial_{x_{k},x_{k^{\prime}}}g(\bm{X}_{T}^{s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N},N})\Big|^{4}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},k,N}X_{T,x_{j}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{k^{\prime},N},k^{\prime},N}\Big|^{4}\Big]}

\displaystyle\leq K(s-t_{m})e^{-2\lambda_{2}(T-s)}e^{-4\lambda_{1}(T-t_{m})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,k^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,N\},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k,k^{\prime}))=\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N^{2\hat{\mathcal{O}}((k,k^{\prime}))}}\frac{1}{N^{2(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,k))+\hat{\mathcal{O}}((j,k^{\prime}))-2))}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+N\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,k^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,N\},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k,k^{\prime}))=\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))+1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N^{2\hat{\mathcal{O}}((k,k^{\prime}))}}\frac{1}{N^{2(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,k))+\hat{\mathcal{O}}((j,k^{\prime}))-2))}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+N^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,k^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,N\},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k,k^{\prime}))=\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))+2\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N^{2\hat{\mathcal{O}}((k,k^{\prime}))}}\frac{1}{N^{2(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,k))+\hat{\mathcal{O}}((j,k^{\prime}))-2))}}\Bigg)

(6.30)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K(s-t_{m})e^{-2\lambda_{2}(T-s)}e^{-4\lambda_{1}(T-t_{m})}}{N^{2\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))}}\cdot\sum_{m=0}^{2}N^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,k^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,N\},\\
\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k,k^{\prime}))=\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))+m\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N^{2m}}

(6.31)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K(s-t_{m})e^{-2\lambda_{2}(T-s)}e^{-4\lambda_{1}(T-t_{m})}}{N^{2\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))}}.

(6.32)

The term (
6.30
) is derived by applying the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, which then requires the
L^{8}
-moments of
X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},k,N}
and
X_{T,x_{j}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{k^{\prime},N},k^{\prime},N}

obtained from Lemma
4.2
(that holds without integrability requirements on
X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},X_{t_{m}}^{k^{\prime},N}
since (
4.1
) is a linear ODE with bounded coefficients starting from either
1
or
0
), and noting we can unify the bounds in Lemma
4.2
as (note that
\lambda_{1}<\lambda
and
\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,k))
is either 0 or 1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\,|X_{T,x_{i}}^{t_{m},X_{t_{m}}^{k,N},k,N}|^{8}\big]\leq\frac{K}{N^{8(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,k))-1)}}e^{-8\lambda_{1}(T-t_{m})}.

To obtain (
6.31
), we used the following bounds, which can be confirmed by checking cases

\displaystyle 2\hat{\mathcal{O}}\big((k,k^{\prime})\big)

\displaystyle+2\big(\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,k))+\hat{\mathcal{O}}((j,k^{\prime}))-2\big)

\displaystyle\geq\begin{cases}2+2(1+\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))-2)=2\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j)),&\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k,k^{\prime}))=\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j)),\\
4+2(1+2-2)=6\geq 2+2\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j)),&\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k,k^{\prime}))=\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))+1,~k\neq k^{\prime},\\
2+2(2+2-2)=6\geq 2+2\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j)),&\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k,k^{\prime}))=\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))+1,~k=k^{\prime},\\
4+2(2+2-2)=8\geq 4+2\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j)),&\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j,k,k^{\prime}))=\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))+2.\end{cases}

In (
6.32
), we used (
5.2
) to ensure the summation term in (
6.31
) is
\mathcal{O}(1)
. We establish bounds for (
6.25
) and (
6.26
) in a similar fashion and obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{eq: uxx term4}+\eqref{eq: uxx term5}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K(s-t_{m})e^{-2\lambda_{4}(T-s)}}{N^{2\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))}}.

(6.33)

Substituting (
6.28
)–(
6.33
) into (
6.22
)–(
6.26
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\partial_{x_{i},x_{j}}^{2}u(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})-\partial_{x_{i},x_{j}}^{2}u(s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\big|^{2}\Big]\leq~\frac{K(s-t_{m})e^{-2\lambda_{4}(T-s)}}{N^{2\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))}}.

(6.34)

Part 2.3: Collecting the estimates for (
6.16
)–(
6.18
).

Consequently, substituting (
6.21
) and (
6.34
) into (
6.16
)–(
6.18
) and using the bounds for the derivatives of the function
B
in (
4.74
), we conclude that
there exists some constant
\lambda_{0}\in(0,\lambda)
such that for all
m
and
s\in[t_{m},t_{m+1}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[

\displaystyle L(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})-L(s,\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N})\Big]

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\Bigg(\frac{\sqrt{s-t_{m}}}{N^{\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))-1}\cdot N}e^{-\lambda_{2}(T-s)}+\frac{\sqrt{s-t_{m}}}{N^{\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))-1}N^{\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))}}e^{-\lambda_{4}(T-s)}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\frac{\sqrt{s-t_{m}}}{N^{\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))-1}\cdot N}e^{-\lambda_{2}(T-s)}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{1/2}e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-s)}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\frac{1}{N^{\hat{\mathcal{O}}((i,j))}}\leq Kh^{1/2}e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-t_{m+1})},

(6.35)

where once again the final inequality arises from recalling (
5.2
).

Part 3: Concluding the proof.

Substituting the results of (
6.14
), (
6.15
), (
6.35
) to (
6.11
)–(
6.13
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m+1}}\Big(L(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})-L(s,\bm{X}_{s}^{N})\Big)\mathrm{d}s\Big]\leq Kh^{3/2}\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-t_{m+1})}\leq Kh^{1/2}.

Therefore, for the left-hand side of (
6.5
), there exists some positive constants
\lambda_{0}\in(0,\lambda),K
such that

\displaystyle h^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}L(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})\Big]\leq Kh^{3/2}+Khe^{-\lambda_{0}T}.

∎

Remark 6.1

(On losing
1/2
in the rate of convergence)
.

Letting
T\rightarrow\infty
in (
6.5
), and temporarily ignoring higher-order remainder terms, we have that the weak error is of order
3/2
. In
[
43
]
this term satisfies the bound (with
K
depending on the dimension
N
)

\displaystyle h^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}L(t_{m},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})\Big]\leq Kh^{2}+Khe^{-\lambda_{0}T},

and thus a weak order of
2
is attained.

The loss of
1/2
in the convergence rate in our estimates occurs in the final step of (
6.14
). In
[
43
]
this term is estimated with the weak error (starting on (
6.11
)), whilst here, we are not able to do so. In fact, to recover the missing
1/2
-rate by following using the arguments
[
43
]
one would need to show that
L
in (
3.7
) satisfies condition (3) of Assumption
2
– see additionally our Remark
3.1
. At present, this is an open question.

6.2
Analysis of residual terms

A close inspection of the proof of the main result in
[
43
]
shows that there are 8 remainder terms which need to be analyzed and we do so in the next lemma.
We will make use of the following helpful abbreviation: for
m\in\{0,\ldots,M-1\}
, we define using (
2.3
) and (
2.6
)

\displaystyle\Delta X_{t_{m}}^{i,N,h}:=

\displaystyle X^{i,N,h}_{t_{m+1}}-X^{i,N,h}_{t_{m}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big(\nabla U(X^{i,N,h}_{t_{m}})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\nabla V(X_{t_{m}}^{i,N,h}-X_{t_{m}}^{j,N,h})\Big)h+\frac{\sigma}{2}(\Delta W_{m}^{i}+\Delta W_{m+1}^{i})

(6.36)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle B_{i}(\bm{X}^{N,h}_{t_{m}})h+\frac{\sigma}{2}(\Delta W_{m}^{i}+\Delta W_{m+1}^{i}).

Further, we define the continuous extension of
\bm{X}^{N,h}_{t_{m}}
: for all
s\in[0,h]

\displaystyle X_{t_{m}+s}^{i,N,h}:=

\displaystyle X^{i,N,h}_{t_{m}}+\Big(\nabla U(X^{i,N,h}_{t_{m}})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\nabla V(X_{t_{m}}^{i,N,h}-X_{t_{m}}^{j,N,h})+\frac{\sigma}{2h}\Delta W_{m}^{i}\Big)s+\frac{\sigma}{2}\Delta W_{m+1,s}^{i}

(6.37)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle X_{t_{m}}^{i,N,h}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Big(B_{i}(\bm{X}^{N,h}_{t_{m}})+\frac{\sigma\Delta W_{m}^{i}}{2h}\Big)s+\frac{\sigma}{2}\Delta W_{m+1,s}^{i},

\displaystyle\Delta W^{i}_{m,s}

\displaystyle={W}^{i}_{t_{m-1}+s}-{W}^{i}_{t_{m-1}},\text{ for }m>0.

(6.38)

Equivalently, we could write

\displaystyle\Delta\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h}

\displaystyle:=\bm{X}^{N,h}_{t_{m+1}}-\bm{X}^{N,h}_{t_{m}}=B(\bm{X}^{N,h}_{t_{m}})h+\frac{\sigma}{2}(\Delta\bm{W}_{m}+\Delta\bm{W}_{m+1}),

\displaystyle\bm{X}_{t_{m}+s}^{N,h}

\displaystyle:=\bm{X}^{N,h}_{t_{m}}+\Big(B(\bm{X}^{N,h}_{t_{m}})+\frac{\sigma\Delta\bm{W}_{m,h}}{2h}\Big)s+\frac{\sigma}{2}\Delta\bm{W}_{m+1,s},

where
\Delta\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h}\coloneqq(\Delta X_{t_{m}}^{1,N,h},\ldots,\Delta X_{t_{m}}^{N,N,h})
,
\Delta\bm{W}_{m}\coloneqq(\Delta W^{1}_{m},\ldots,\Delta W^{N}_{m})
and
\Delta\bm{W}_{m,s}\coloneqq(\Delta W^{1}_{m,s},\ldots,\Delta W^{N}_{m,s})
(for
m>0
).
Instead of dealing with the expression above, we rewrite the scheme in a different way (see
[
43
, p7]
). For
m\in\{0,\ldots,M-1\}
and
i\in\{1,\dots,N\}
, with
\hat{X}^{i,N,h}_{t_{0}}=X^{i,N}_{t_{0}}
, define

\displaystyle\hat{X}^{i,N,h}_{t_{m+1}}

\displaystyle=\hat{X}^{i,N,h}_{t_{m}}+\sigma\Delta W_{m}^{i}

(6.39)

\displaystyle-\Big(\nabla U(\hat{X}^{i,N,h}_{t_{m}}+\frac{\sigma}{2}\Delta W_{m}^{i})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\nabla V\big(\hat{X}_{t_{m}}^{i,N,h}+\frac{\sigma}{2}\Delta W_{m}^{i}-\hat{X}_{t_{m}}^{j,N,h}-\frac{\sigma}{2}\Delta W_{m}^{j}\big)\Big)h,

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{X}}^{N,h}_{t_{m+1}}

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\hat{\bm{X}}^{N,h}_{t_{m}}+\sigma\Delta\bm{W}_{m}+B\big(\hat{\bm{X}}^{N,h}_{t_{m}}+\frac{\sigma}{2}\Delta\bm{W}_{m}\big)h,

so that
X^{i,N,h}_{t_{m}}=\hat{X}^{i,N,h}_{t_{m}}+\sigma\Delta W_{m}^{i}/2
, for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}

(or
\bm{X}^{N,h}_{t_{m}}=\hat{\bm{X}}^{N,h}_{t_{m}}+\sigma\Delta\bm{W}_{m}/2
).

Now, for
s\in[0,h)
and
m\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
, we define the following auxiliary process

\displaystyle\overline{X}^{i,N,h}_{t_{m-1}+s}

\displaystyle=\hat{X}^{i,N,h}_{t_{m}}+\frac{\sigma}{2}\Delta W_{m,s}^{i},\quad\overline{X}^{i,N,h}_{t_{m-1}}=\hat{X}^{i,N,h}_{t_{m}},\quad

(6.40)

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{X}}_{t_{m-1}+s}^{N,h}

\displaystyle=\hat{\bm{X}}_{{t_{m}}}^{N,h}+\frac{\sigma}{2}\Delta\bm{W}_{m,s},\quad\overline{\bm{X}}_{t_{m-1}}=\hat{\bm{X}}_{{t_{m}}}^{N,h},

where we remark that this form is used in the proof of Lemma
6.2
(e.g., in (
6.47
)). The moment stability of these auxiliary schemes is discussed in the appendix, see Lemma
A.5
.
We refer the reader to
[
56
]
for different versions of such schemes, coined there
‘postprocessed schemes’, achieving higher-order weak convergence in the ergodic setting.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let Assumption
2
hold and let
\xi\in L^{10}(\Omega,\mathbb{R})
. Then for the remainder term
R
in (
6.1
), there exists a positive constant
K
independent of
h,T,M
and
N
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}R(t_{m},\bm{X}^{N,h}_{t_{m}})\right]\leq Kh^{3/2}.

Proof.

Recall that the remainder term
r(t_{m},\cdot)
in
[
43
, Equation (3.17)]
(and corresponding to our
R(t_{m},\cdot)
term in (
6.1
)) is given as a linear combination of remainders denoted as
h^{3}r_{i}(t_{m},\cdot)
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,8\}
and appearing in Equations (3.8), (3.10)–(3.16) in
[
43
, p7-9]
, where they correspond to the remainder terms of the Itô-Taylor expansions in (
3.7
) – we refer to the terms
\{R^{i}_{t_{m}}\}_{i=1}^{8}
in this proof and Appendix
A.5
for similar detailed forms.
In our case, we derive bounds for
\mathbb{E}[R(t_{m},\bm{X}^{N,h}_{t_{m}})]\leq\sum_{i=1}^{8}|\mathbb{E}[R^{i}_{t_{m}}]|
, where each
R^{i}_{t_{m}}
term corresponds to the
h^{3}r_{i}(t_{m},\cdot)
terms (
i\in\{1,\ldots,8\}
) of
[
43
, p7-9]
.

It may not be immediately transparent how the
h^{3}r_{i}(t_{m},\cdot)
terms correspond to our
R_{t_{m}}^{i}
terms. We explicitly present the derivations of
\mathbb{E}[R_{t_{m}}^{1}]
,
\mathbb{E}[R_{t_{m}}^{4}]
and
\mathbb{E}[R_{t_{m}}^{6}]
, which we feel encapsulate the techniques and proof methodology. The derivation of the residual terms
R_{t_{m}}^{2},\,R_{t_{m}}^{3},\,R_{t_{m}}^{5},\,R_{t_{m}}^{7},\,R_{t_{m}}^{8}
, can be found in Appendix
A.5
. In this proof, let
K
be a constant independent of
m,M,N,T,h
which may change from line to line.

Part 1:

Recall the
\Delta X
notation introduced in (
6.36
). The remainder term
h^{3}r_{1}
in Equation (3.8) of
[
43
, p7]
is established from the Taylor expansion with Lagrange’s form of the remainder term. That is, for all
m
, there exists some
\rho_{m}\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[R^{1}_{t_{m}}\big]

\displaystyle=K\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{5}^{N}}\Delta X^{\gamma_{1},N,h}_{t_{m}}\dots\Delta X^{\gamma_{5},N,h}_{t_{m}}~\partial^{5}_{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{5}}u(t_{m+1},\bm{X}_{m,{\rho_{m}}}^{N,h})\bigg]

where
\bm{X}_{m,{\rho_{m}}}^{N,h}:={\rho_{m}}\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h}+(1-{\rho_{m}})\bm{X}_{t_{m+1}}^{N,h}
.

We deal with
|\mathbb{E}[R^{1}_{t_{m}}]|
via Hölder’s inequality to isolate the
\Delta X
terms from the derivatives of
u
term,

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[R^{1}_{t_{m}}\big]\big|

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{5}^{N}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\Delta X^{\gamma_{1},N,h}_{t_{m}}\dots\Delta X^{\gamma_{5},N,h}_{t_{m}}\big|^{2}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[~\Big|\partial^{5}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{5}}}~u(t_{m+1},\bm{X}_{m,{\rho_{m}}}^{N,h})\Big|^{2}\Big]}

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{5/2}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{5}^{N}}e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-t_{m+1})}N^{-\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)}\leq Kh^{5/2}e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-t_{m+1})},

(6.41)

which holds for some
\lambda_{0}\in(0,\lambda)
. We derive (
6.41
) using Lemma
5.3
to treat the derivatives of
u
, while for the
\Delta X
terms, we use their explicit forms (
6.36
) and the fact that
\nabla U,\nabla V
are of linear growth. In particular, the assumption
\xi\in L^{10}(\Omega,\mathbb{R})
, allows to use Proposition
2.1
and Proposition
2.2
to control the
L^{10}
-moments of the processes. The moment properties of the increments extract the leading order
h
terms; providing the
h^{5/2}
leading order. Note that for
R^{1}_{t_{m}}
we incur a loss of
h^{1/2}
in the leading term while in
[
43
]
there is no such loss – this issue has already appeared in the proof of Lemma
6.1
and is discussed in Remark
6.1
(and Remark
3.1
). Critically,
[
43
]
has a
|\cdot|_{\infty}
- norm bound for the 5-th derivative of
u
while here we are only able to bound it in expectation. We are forced to use Hölder’s inequality.

Similar to
[
43
, Equation (3.12)]
, we show how the
R_{t_{m}}^{4}
(corresponding to
r_{4}(t_{m},\cdot)h^{3}
in
[
43
, Equation (3.12)]
) is generated and give the exact form of
\mathbb{E}[R_{t_{m}}^{4}]
. Expanding out the increments
\Delta X^{\gamma_{i},N,h}_{t_{m}}
,
i\in\{1,2,3\}
, and recall the definition of
\Delta W_{m,2h}
in (
6.38
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Delta X^{\gamma_{1},N,h}_{t_{m}}\Delta X^{\gamma_{2},N,h}_{t_{m}}\Delta X^{\gamma_{3},N,h}_{t_{m}}~\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}u(t_{m+1},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{1}}\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{2}}\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{3}}~\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}u(t_{m+1},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})\Big]

(6.42)

\displaystyle+Kh\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\Big[B_{\gamma_{1}}({\bm{X}}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{2}}\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{3}}~\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}u(t_{m+1},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})\Big]

(6.43)

\displaystyle+Kh^{2}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\Big[B_{\gamma_{1}}({\bm{X}}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})B_{\gamma_{2}}({\bm{X}}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{3}}~\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}u(t_{m+1},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})\Big]

(6.44)

\displaystyle+Kh^{3}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\Big[B_{\gamma_{1}}({\bm{X}}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})B_{\gamma_{2}}({\bm{X}}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})B_{\gamma_{3}}({\bm{X}}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})~\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}u(t_{m+1},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})\Big].

(6.45)

We first deal with the term (
6.42
), by applying an Itô-Taylor expansion (see
[
37
, Section 5.1, p.163–164]
) around
\hat{\bm{X}}_{{t_{m}}}^{N,h}
to obtain

(
6.42
)

\displaystyle=K\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{1}}\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{2}}\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{3}}~\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}u(t_{m+1},\hat{\bm{X}}_{{t_{m}}}^{N,h})\Big]

(6.46)

\displaystyle+K\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{4}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\partial_{x_{\gamma_{1}}}\bigg(\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{2}}\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{3}}\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{4}}~\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{4}}}u(t_{m+1},\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h})\bigg)\mathrm{d}W^{\gamma_{1}}_{q_{1}}\bigg]

(6.47)

\displaystyle+K\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{4}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\partial^{2}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{1}}}\bigg(\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{2}}\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{3}}\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{4}}~\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{4}}}u(t_{m+1},\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h})\bigg)\mathrm{d}{q_{1}}\bigg].

(6.48)

One observes that
\eqref{eq: r4 term 1.1}=0
since for
\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}
, regardless of the value of
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)
, there will always be an odd power of a Brownian increment
\Delta W_{m,2h}
presented in (
6.38
), independent of the Brownian increment contained in the
\hat{\bm{X}}_{{t_{m}}}^{N,h}
term. The term (
6.48
) however, does not vanish since the
\Delta W_{m,2h}
term is not independent of
\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}
, because the latter contains the Brownian increment
W_{q_{1}}-W_{t_{m}}
.

Applying a further Itô-Taylor expansion around
\hat{\bm{X}}_{t_{m}}^{N,h}
, and splitting the zeroth order term into the cases
\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)\in\{1,2\}
yields

\displaystyle\eqref{eq: r4 term 1.2}=

\displaystyle Kh^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\partial^{4}_{x_{i},x_{i},x_{i},x_{i}}u(t_{m+1},\hat{\bm{X}}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})\bigg]+Kh^{2}\sum_{i,j=1,~i\neq j}^{N}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\partial^{4}_{x_{i},x_{i},x_{j},x_{j}}u(t_{m+1},\hat{\bm{X}}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})\bigg]

(6.49)

\displaystyle+K\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{5}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\int_{t_{m}}^{q_{1}}\partial_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}}}\bigg(\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{3}}\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{4}}\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{5}}~\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{4}},x_{\gamma_{4}},x_{\gamma_{5}}}u(t_{m+1},\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{2}}^{N,h})\bigg)\mathrm{d}W^{\gamma_{1}}_{q_{2}}\ \mathrm{d}W^{\gamma_{2}}_{q_{1}}\bigg]

(6.50)

\displaystyle+K\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{5}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\int_{t_{m}}^{q_{1}}\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}}}\bigg(\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{3}}\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{4}}\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{5}}~\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{4}},x_{\gamma_{4}},x_{\gamma_{5}}}u(t_{m+1},\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{2}}^{N,h})\bigg)\mathrm{d}{q_{2}}\ \mathrm{d}W^{\gamma_{2}}_{q_{1}}\bigg],

(6.51)

where the two summation terms in (
6.49
) correspond to the second and third summation in
[
43
, (3.12)]
and they do not contribute to the remainder term
R^{4}_{t_{m}}
.

Similarly for (
6.43
), we have

\displaystyle\eqref{eq: r4 term2}=Kh\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Big[B_{i}(\hat{\bm{X}}_{{t_{m}}}^{N,h})\partial^{3}_{x_{i},x_{j},x_{j}}u(t_{m+1},\hat{\bm{X}}_{{t_{m}}}^{N,h})\Big]

(6.52)

\displaystyle+Kh\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{4}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\partial_{x_{\gamma_{1}}}\bigg(B_{\gamma_{2}}(\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h})\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{3}}\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{4}}~\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{4}}}u(t_{m+1},\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h})\bigg)\mathrm{d}W^{\gamma_{1}}_{q_{1}}\bigg]

(6.53)

\displaystyle+Kh\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{4}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\partial^{2}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{1}}}\bigg(B_{\gamma_{2}}(\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h})\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{3}}\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{4}}~\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{4}}}u(t_{m+1},\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h})\bigg)\mathrm{d}{q_{1}}\bigg],

(6.54)

where (
6.52
) corresponds to the first summation in
[
43
, (3.12)]
, thus also does not contribute to
R_{t_{m}}^{4}
. Leimkuhler et al. isolates and separates the terms (
6.49
) and (
6.52
) from
h^{3}r_{4}
, since these terms cleverly cancel with other corresponding lower order terms in the expansion of other
r_{i}
’s when everything is summed over.

As for (
6.44
), we have

\displaystyle\eqref{eq: r4 term3}=Kh^{2}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\Big[B_{\gamma_{1}}(\hat{\bm{X}}_{{t_{m}}}^{N,h})B_{\gamma_{2}}(\hat{\bm{X}}_{{t_{m}}}^{N,h})\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{3}}~\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}u(t_{m+1},\hat{\bm{X}}_{{t_{m}}}^{N,h})\Big]

(6.55)

\displaystyle+Kh^{2}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{4}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\partial_{x_{\gamma_{1}}}\bigg(B_{\gamma_{2}}(\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h})B_{\gamma_{3}}(\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h})\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{4}}~\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{4}}}u(t_{m+1},\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h})\bigg)\mathrm{d}W^{\gamma_{1}}_{q_{1}}\bigg]

(6.56)

\displaystyle+Kh^{2}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{4}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\partial^{2}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{1}}}\bigg(B_{\gamma_{2}}(\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h})B_{\gamma_{3}}(\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h})\Delta W_{m,2h}^{\gamma_{4}}~\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{4}}}u(t_{m+1},\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h})\bigg)\mathrm{d}{q_{1}}\bigg],

(6.57)

where the first term in this expansion (
6.55
) is zero, following the same reasoning as for (
6.46
).

For the residual term
R^{4}_{t_{m}}
, we have that via direct application of Hölder’s inequality, Itô’s isometry and Fubini’s Theorem, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\big[R^{4}_{t_{m}}\big]=\eqref{eq: r4 term4}+\eqref{eq: r4 term 1.3}+\eqref{eq: R4 comb1}+\eqref{eq: R4 comb2}+\eqref{eq: R4 comb4}+\eqref{eq: R4 comb5}+\eqref{eq: R4 comb6}+\eqref{eq: R4 comb7}

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{3}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\Big[B_{\gamma_{1}}({\bm{X}}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})B_{\gamma_{2}}({\bm{X}}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})B_{\gamma_{3}}({\bm{X}}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})~\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}u(t_{m+1},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+Kh^{3/2}~\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}~\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{4}^{N}}\partial^{5}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{4}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})~\bigg|^{2}~~\bigg]\bigg)^{1/2}~\mathrm{d}q_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+Kh^{2}\bigg(\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{5}^{N}}\partial^{5}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{5}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})~\bigg|^{2}~~\bigg]~\mathrm{d}q_{1}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+Kh^{2}\bigg(\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{5}^{N}}\partial^{6}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{4}},x_{\gamma_{4}},x_{\gamma_{5}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})~\bigg|^{2}~~\bigg]~~\mathrm{d}q_{1}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+Kh^{2}\bigg(\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{4}^{N}}\partial_{x_{\gamma_{1}}}\bigg(B_{\gamma_{2}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{4}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\bigg)~\bigg|^{2}~~\bigg]~\mathrm{d}q_{1}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+Kh^{3/2}\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{4}^{N}}\partial^{2}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{1}}}\bigg(B_{\gamma_{2}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{4}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\bigg)~\bigg|^{2}~~\bigg]\bigg)^{1/2}~\mathrm{d}q_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+Kh^{5/2}\bigg(\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{4}^{N}}\partial_{x_{\gamma_{1}}}\bigg(B_{\gamma_{2}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})B_{\gamma_{3}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{4}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\bigg)~\bigg|^{2}~~\bigg]~\mathrm{d}q_{1}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+Kh^{5/2}\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{4}^{N}}\partial^{2}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{1}}}\bigg(B_{\gamma_{2}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})B_{\gamma_{3}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{4}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\bigg)~\bigg|^{2}~~\bigg]~\bigg)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}q_{1}.

As for
R_{t_{m}}^{6}
, recalling the definition of the operator
\mathcal{L}_{N}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{N}u

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big(B_{i}\partial_{x_{i}}u+\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}\partial^{2}_{x_{i},x_{i}}u\Big),

the remainder term
r_{6}(t_{m},\cdot)h^{3}
in
[
43
, Equation (3.14)]
(apply
\mathcal{L}_{N}
three times) corresponds to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[R^{6}_{t_{m}}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\int_{t_{m}}^{q_{1}}\int_{t_{m}}^{q_{2}}(\mathcal{L}_{N})^{3}u(t_{m+1},{\bm{X}}_{q_{3}}^{N,h})\mathrm{d}q_{3}~\mathrm{d}q_{2}~\mathrm{d}q_{1}~\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\int_{t_{m}}^{q_{1}}\int_{t_{m}}^{q_{2}}\Bigg(K\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}B_{\gamma_{1}}({\bm{X}}_{q_{3}}^{N,h})B_{\gamma_{2}}({\bm{X}}_{q_{3}}^{N,h})B_{\gamma_{3}}({\bm{X}}_{q_{3}}^{N,h})\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}u(t_{m+1},{\bm{X}}_{q_{3}}^{N,h})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+K\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}\partial^{2}_{x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}\bigg(B_{\gamma_{1}}({\bm{X}}_{q_{3}}^{N,h})B_{\gamma_{2}}({\bm{X}}_{q_{3}}^{N,h})\partial^{2}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}}}u(t_{m+1},{\bm{X}}_{q_{3}}^{N,h})\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+K\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}\partial^{4}_{x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}\bigg(B_{\gamma_{1}}({\bm{X}}_{q_{3}}^{N,h})\partial_{x_{\gamma_{1}}}u(t_{m+1},{\bm{X}}_{q_{3}}^{N,h})\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+K\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{6}^{N},\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)\leq 3}\partial^{6}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{6}}}u(t_{m+1},{\bm{X}}_{q_{3}}^{N,h})\Bigg)\mathrm{d}q_{3}~\mathrm{d}q_{2}~\mathrm{d}q_{1}~\bigg].

Once again, a direct application of Hölder’s inequality and Fubini’s Theorem

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[R^{6}_{t_{m}}\big]\big|

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{2}\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|~\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}B_{\gamma_{1}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})B_{\gamma_{2}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})B_{\gamma_{3}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\partial^{3}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}\partial^{2}_{x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}\bigg(B_{\gamma_{1}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})B_{\gamma_{2}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\partial^{2}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}\partial^{4}_{x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}\bigg(B_{\gamma_{1}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\partial_{x_{\gamma_{1}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{6}^{N},\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)\leq 3}\partial^{6}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{6}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})~\bigg|^{2}\bigg]\bigg)^{1/2}~\mathrm{d}q_{1}.

Part 2: Other remainder terms.

We now present the dominations of the other residual terms, whose explicit expressions can be found in Appendix
A.5
. These, once again, are a result of applying Hölder’s inequality, Itô’s isometry and Fubini’s Theorem.
We have

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[R^{2}_{t_{m}}\big]\big|

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{2}\bigg(~\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{5}^{N}}\partial^{5}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{5}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})~\bigg|^{2}~\bigg]~\mathrm{d}q_{1}~\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+Kh^{5/2}\bigg(~\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{6}^{N}}\partial^{6}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{6}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})~\bigg|^{2}~\bigg]~\mathrm{d}q_{1}~\bigg)^{1/2},

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[R^{3}_{t_{m}}\big]\big|

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{2}\bigg(~\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}\partial_{x_{\gamma_{1}}}\bigg(\partial_{x_{\gamma_{3}}}B_{\gamma_{2}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\partial^{2}_{x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\bigg)~\bigg|^{2}~\bigg]~\mathrm{d}q_{1}~\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+Kh^{2}\bigg(~\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}\partial^{2}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{1}}}\bigg(\partial_{x_{\gamma_{3}}}B_{\gamma_{2}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\partial^{2}_{x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\bigg)~\bigg|^{2}~\bigg]~\mathrm{d}q_{1}~\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+Kh^{2}\bigg(~\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}\partial_{x_{\gamma_{1}}}\bigg(B_{\gamma_{2}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\partial^{2}_{x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\bigg)~\bigg|^{2}~\bigg]~\mathrm{d}q_{1}~\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+Kh^{2}\bigg(~\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{3}^{N}}\partial^{2}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{1}}}\bigg(B_{\gamma_{2}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\partial^{2}_{x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{3}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\bigg)~\bigg|^{2}~\bigg]~\mathrm{d}q_{1}~\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+Kh^{2}\bigg(~\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{5}^{N}}\partial^{5}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{5}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})~\bigg|^{2}~\bigg]~\mathrm{d}q_{1}~\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+Kh^{5/2}\bigg(~\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{6}^{N}}\partial^{6}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{6}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})~\bigg|^{2}~\bigg]~\mathrm{d}q_{1}~\bigg)^{1/2},

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[R^{5}_{t_{m}}\big]\big|

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{5/2}\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{4}^{N}}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\partial^{4}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{4}}}u(t_{m+1},\bm{X}_{t_{m}}^{N,h})~\bigg|^{2}~\bigg]~\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+Kh^{2}\bigg(~\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{5}^{N}}\partial^{5}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{5}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})~\bigg|^{2}~\bigg]~\mathrm{d}q_{1}~\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+Kh^{2}\bigg(\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}~\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{5}^{N}}\partial^{6}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{1}},x_{\gamma_{2}},x_{\gamma_{3}},x_{\gamma_{4}},x_{\gamma_{5}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})~\bigg|^{2}~\bigg]~\mathrm{d}q_{1}~\bigg)^{1/2},

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[R^{7}_{t_{m}}\big]\big|

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{2}\bigg(~\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|~\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{6}^{N},\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)\in\{4,5\}}\partial^{6}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{6}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})~\bigg|^{2}~\bigg]~\mathrm{d}q_{1}~\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+Kh^{2}\bigg(~\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|~\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{5}^{N},\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)=4}\partial^{6}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{6}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})~\bigg|^{2}~\bigg]~\mathrm{d}q_{1}~\bigg)^{1/2},

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[R^{8}_{t_{m}}\big]\big|

\displaystyle\leq Kh^{2}\bigg(~\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|~\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{5}^{N},\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)\leq 3}\partial^{5}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{5}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})~\bigg|^{2}~\bigg]\mathrm{d}q_{1}~\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+Kh^{2}\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}~\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|~\sum_{\gamma\in\Pi_{6}^{N},\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)\leq 3}\partial^{6}_{x_{\gamma_{1}},\dots,x_{\gamma_{6}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})~\bigg|^{2}~\bigg]\bigg)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}q_{1}~

\displaystyle+Kh^{2}\bigg(~\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m}+h}

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}\bigg[~\bigg|~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,\beta\in\bigcup_{k=0}^{5}\Pi_{k}^{N},~\hat{\mathcal{O}}(\alpha\bigcup\beta)=3\\
|\alpha|+|\beta|=5,~|\beta|\geq 2\end{subarray}}\partial^{|\alpha|}_{x_{\alpha_{1}},\dots,x_{\alpha_{|\alpha|}}}B_{\beta_{1}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})~\partial^{|\beta|}_{x_{\beta_{1}},\dots,x_{\beta_{|\beta|}}}u(t_{m+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\bm{X}}_{q_{1}}^{N,h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\bigg)~~\bigg|^{2}~\bigg]\mathrm{d}q_{1}~\bigg)^{1/2}.

Similar to (
6.41
) and the proof of Lemma
6.1
, we can use the linear growth of
B_{j}
in combination with the assumption
\xi\in L^{10}(\Omega,\mathbb{R})
and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality to establish
L^{10}
-estimates of the
B_{j}
(see, Proposition
2.1
and Lemma
A.5
) which, for instance, are employed in the estimation of
R^{3}_{t_{m}},R^{5}_{t_{m}},R^{8}_{t_{m}}
. An application of Lemma
5.3
allows to control the moments of derivatives of the solution,
u
, to the Kolmogorov backward equation. Hence, all the above expectations are well-defined and bounded by
K(h^{5/2}+h^{3})e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-t_{m+1})}
.

Part 3: Collecting the estimates.
For the eight residual terms
R^{1}_{t_{m}},\ldots,R^{8}_{t_{m}}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{8}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[R^{i}_{t_{m}}\big]\big|\leq K(h^{5/2}+h^{3})e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-t_{m+1})}.

Combining all of the above estimates, we conclude

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}\mathbb{E}\big[R(t_{m},\bm{X}^{N,h}_{t_{m}})\big]\leq\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}\sum_{i=1}^{8}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[R^{i}_{t_{m}}\big]\big|\leq\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}K(h^{5/2}+h^{3})e^{-\lambda_{0}(T-t_{m+1})}\leq Kh^{3/2}.

∎

7
Numerical illustration

We illustrate the performance of the non-Markovian Euler scheme (
2.6
) with a simple linear MV-SDE example. Consider the following mean-field equation:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{d}X_{t}=\Big(-\alpha\big(X_{t}-\mathbb{E}[X_{t}]\big)-X_{t}\Big)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma\mathrm{d}W_{t}},\quad X_{0}\in L^{10}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}),

(7.1)

where
\alpha,\sigma>0
. Explicit calculations yield
\mathbb{E}[X_{t}]=\mathbb{E}[X_{0}]e^{-t}
and thus this process admits the following stationary distribution

\displaystyle\mu^{*}(x)=Z\exp\Big(-\frac{\alpha+1}{\sigma^{2}}x^{2}\Big),

(7.2)

where
Z
is the renormalization constant such that
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mu^{*}(x)\mathrm{d}x=1
.
We use the following recipe to compute errors in the numerical experiments.

1.

Choose a large enough domain
[a,b]
such that
\big(F_{\textrm{cdf}}(b)-F_{\textrm{cdf}}(a)\big)>1-10^{-6}
, where
F_{\textrm{cdf}}
denotes the cumulative density function (CDF) of the invariant distribution
\mu
of (
7.2
).

2.

Split the domain
[a,b]
into
N_{\textrm{bins}}
equally spaced bins and compute the true density in each bin. These values are denoted
(\mu_{i}^{\textrm{true}})_{i\in\{1,\dots,N_{\textrm{bins}}\}}
and obtained using numerical integration.
Additionally, the first bin and the value
\mu_{1}^{\textrm{true}}
takes the interval
(-\infty,a]
into account while the last bin (and the value
\mu_{N_{\textrm{bins}}}^{\textrm{true}}
) takes the interval
[b,\infty)
. In this way, one has

\sum_{i=1}^{N_{\textrm{bins}}}\mu_{i}^{\textrm{true}}=1
.

3.

We simulate
N
particles up to time
T
using different time-stepping schemes
and compute an approximation of the density (using a histogram approach) denoted by
(\mu_{i}^{\textrm{proxy}})_{i\in\{1,\dots,N_{\textrm{bins}}\}}
based on the simulated paths.

We choose
f(x)=x\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{x\geq 0}
for
x\in\mathbb{R}
as the test function in the weak error test in Figure
2
.

4.

As in
[
43
]
, we compute the relative entropy error and the
L_{2}
-Error as

Relative Entropy Error

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N_{\textrm{bins}}}\mu_{i}^{\textrm{true}}\ln\Big(\frac{\mu_{i}^{\textrm{true}}}{\mu_{i}^{\textrm{proxy}}}\Big)

L_{2}
-Error

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N_{\textrm{bins}}}|\mu_{i}^{\textrm{true}}-\mu_{i}^{\textrm{proxy}}|^{2}}.

5.

We also show a PoC result by computing the
L_{2}
-Error for different sizes
N
of particles with fixed time
T
and timestep
h
(chosen independently of
T,M,N
).

The goal of this simple example is to simulate the IPS associated with (
7.1
) up to
T=9
using the classical Euler method and the non-Markovian Euler method, respectively, and compare the results to (
7.2
). Following the recipe above, we set the domain as
[a,b]=[-1.8,1.8]
and split it into
72
bins.

(a)

Rel. Entropy Error

(b)

L_{2}
-Error

(c)

PoC (log-)
L_{2}
-Error,
T=9

Figure 1
:
Simulation of the linear MV-SDE (
7.1
) with
\alpha=0.5,\sigma=0.8
,
N=10^{7},h=0.16
, and
X_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}(\pi,1)
(a normal distribution with mean value
\pi
and variance
1
). Both schemes run on the exact same samples of the initial condition and Brownian increments.
(a) Entropy Error of the Euler method and non-Markovian method in log-scale over time.
(b)
L_{2}
-Error of the Euler method and non-Markovian method in log-scale over time.
(c)
L_{2}
-Error in particle size
N
of the Euler method and non-Markovian method in log-scale with respect to different number of particles
N
at
T=9
.

Figure
1
(a) and (b) show that the non-Markovian method (uniformly) outperforms the Euler method in the approximation of the stationary distribution
\mu
in both error metrics and the difference in performance becomes more evident as time increases. This is expected from the result in Theorem
3.3
, since the first order term decays exponentially as
T
increases. As in
[
43
, Fig.2]
the numerical errors of the methods plateau as
T
increases with the non-Markovian method performing several orders of magnitude better.

Figure
1
(c) shows (for a fixed timestep
h=0.04
) that the PoC
L_{2}
-Error of the non-Markovian method decays consistently as
N
increases with a rate of approximately
0.5
(the expected strong PoC
\mathcal{O}(1/\sqrt{N})
rate). The error of the Euler method plateaus for
N>10^{5}
as the error from the time-discretization dominates the particle error; see also Table
2
below for more information.

(a)

, Slope =
1.02

, Slope =
0.92

, Slope =

, and

(a normal distribution with mean value

and variance
1
) with different choices of time step
h\in\{0.005,0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1\}
at different time
T\in\{1,3,5\}
; the simulation at
T=7
or
T=9
is not different from that in (c) at
T=5
, we thus do not present them.
Both schemes run on the exact same samples of the initial condition and Brownian increments.
We show the slope of the regression line of the non-Markovian method.

For Figure
2
, we take for the weak error the test functional
\mathbb{E}[X_{T}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{X_{T}>0}]
– this is a non-symmetric map as
\mathbb{E}[X_{T}]=0
since
X_{T}
follows a symmetric distribution with zero mean.
As the time increases, we see the weak error rate also increase from around
1.0
to
1.5
. An improvement in the weak error rate is observed when increasing the number of particles from
10^{5}
to
10^{7}
, as shown in later results. Nevertheless, the non-Markovian method outperforms the Euler method in terms of weak convergence.
